# RFC 4271 - A Border Gateway Protocol 4 (BGP-4)

BGP-4 base protocol. Every requirement listed for RFC 4271, the tests linked to it, and their recorded reviews. This summary is included in the automatic compliance check, ./le rfc check.

## Overview

### Positive

what Ze has

| Measure | Value | Count | What it means |
|---|---:|---|---|
| Tested both ways | 122 | of 133 mandatory requirements | a positive test checks that Ze does what the requirement demands and a negative one checks that it refuses what the requirement forbids |
| One polarity plus reason | 2 | of 133 mandatory requirements | the requirement admits no counter-case, so one polarity plus a recorded reason supplies the complete test coverage required |
| Tests shown to detect a fault | 413 | 413 of 537 tests linked to requirements | each test was observed to fail when the code it checks was deliberately broken. The test, its stated claim, and the relevant code are checked against the saved record for changes; the deliberate fault is not run again. Having both positive and negative tests verifies a requirement only after both tests have been shown to detect a fault |

### Negative

what Ze owes

| Measure | Value | Count | What it means |
|---|---:|---|---|
| One polarity, unexcused | 2 | of 133 mandatory requirements | one direction is tested, the other is neither tested nor excused, and nothing states which |
| Partial test coverage | 4 | of 133 mandatory requirements | scoped tests exist; the remaining obligation is unmet or unverified, with zero whole-requirement credit |
| Declared gap or no test | 3 | of 133 mandatory requirements | the requirements the summary declares a gap for, which say what Ze does not do yet, plus the ones no test carries and no annotation excuses. The at-a-glance table lists them apart: declared gaps (at every level), and mandatory requirements with no test and no annotation |
| Audit verdicts | 117 | of 133 mandatory requirements reviewed | 7 weak, wrong or unimplemented, 42 no longer current. Each is named below under its own requirement id |

### Neutral

measures that are neither good news nor bad

| Measure | Value | Count | What it means |
|---|---:|---|---|
| Out of scope | 0 | of 133 mandatory requirements | an obligation that does not bind Ze, counted once its evidence passes. A {not-applicable} annotation says it never bound; a {feature-declined} annotation says its condition is an optional feature Ze does not offer, and quotes the RFC sentence that makes it optional. An annotation whose evidence fails is not counted here: the figures count it as exclusion unchecked. This classification records applicability and forms part of the total accounted for by the cards on this page |
| Not applicable | 0 | of 133 mandatory requirements | a {not-applicable} annotation says the obligation does not bind Ze. No test is owed only once its evidence passes; one whose evidence fails stays owed, and the figures count it as exclusion unchecked. It remains in the total that these cards add up to |
| Met below Ze | 0 | of 133 mandatory requirements | a {lower-layer} annotation says a layer under Ze performs the behavior, on state Ze installs into that layer, and names the producer that installs it. The obligation binds Ze and stays in the MUST bar, outside Ze verified: it is OS verified once a tagged test checks that layer's behavior on the state Ze installs, with a recorded break, and an OS testing gap until then |
| Optional feature declined | 0 | of 133 mandatory requirements | a {feature-declined} annotation says the obligation is conditional on a feature the RFC makes optional and Ze does not offer, and it quotes the sentence that makes it optional. The condition is false, so nothing is owed once that quote passes its evidence check; one whose quote fails stays owed, and the figures count it as exclusion unchecked. It remains in the total that these cards add up to |
| Linux cannot do this | 0 | of 133 mandatory requirements | Linux cannot provide the primitive this requirement needs. The obligation still binds Ze and stays unmet, but as a limit of the kernel, not of Ze's code: once its limit entry is verified, the row is not owed (OS blocked) and is left out of the MUST bar; one whose entry fails stays owed, and the figures count it as not built. It remains in the total that these cards add up to and earns no test verification credit |

### Totals

what the cards above are counted against

| Measure | Value | Count | What it means |
|---|---:|---|---|
| Mandatory requirements (MUST level) | 133 | of 167 requirements this summary lists, at all levels | The RFC's MUST, MUST NOT, SHALL, SHALL NOT and REQUIRED requirements included in the automatic checks: the compliance check (./le rfc check) holds Ze to each of them. This is the total being assessed; the counts above show the recorded status of those requirements. |

The 9 counts marked as a part above are the whole of the 133 mandatory requirements: they add up to 133. Tests shown to detect a fault counts individual tests rather than requirements, so its count is separate.

A color names what the measure MEANS, not how well Ze scores on it. Green is a good outcome at any value, red is a bad one, and neither a population nor a scope count is an outcome, so both take no color. The number under the label is what says how far Ze has got. An outlined card with no colored edge, under Totals, is a total the other cards are counted against, not a measure.

| Card | Tone here | Why that color |
|---|---|---|
| Out of scope | neutral | no color: an obligation that never bound Ze is neither an achievement nor a failure, and counting it either way would be a claim |
| Tested both ways | ok | green at every value: a test pair is the outcome this gate exists to produce, and the count under the label is what says how far Ze has got |
| One polarity plus reason | ok | green at every value: where no counter-case exists, one polarity IS the complete answer, and a recorded reason is what the gate demands beside it |
| One polarity, unexcused | bad | green at zero, RED above it: one test direction is missing without a recorded reason |
| Partial test coverage | bad | green at zero, RED above it: testing one clause does not verify the whole requirement |
| Declared gap or no test | bad | green at zero, RED above it: a binding obligation that nothing shows Ze meets is owed, whether or not a reason is stated |
| Not applicable | neutral | no color: an obligation that never bound Ze is neither an achievement nor a failure, and counting it either way would be a claim |
| Met below Ze | neutral | no color: an obligation met below Ze is neither a test Ze wrote nor work Ze owes, and the two green counts above are what says how much Ze's own tests verify |
| Optional feature declined | neutral | no color: an obligation whose condition Ze never meets is neither an achievement nor a failure. The absent FEATURE is disclosed on the RFC's own status row, as an implementation gap a later scope decision can revisit |
| Linux cannot do this | neutral | neutral at every value: an evidenced platform limit is neither test verification nor a scope exemption |
| Tests shown to detect a fault | ok | green at every value: these tests have been shown to detect a deliberate fault. This card counts individual tests separately, so its total cannot be added to the requirement counts above |
| Audit verdicts | bad | RED on the first weak, wrong or unimplemented verdict, amber while a verdict is no longer current or a mandatory requirement is unreviewed, green when every one is judged sound and current |
| Mandatory requirements (MUST level) | outlined | outlined, with no colored edge: it is the total the other cards are counted against, not a measure, and a larger one is neither good news nor bad |

## Automatic checks

Included in automatic checks: A Border Gateway Protocol 4 (BGP-4): Section 5.1.4 requires a local-configuration mechanism that removes MULTI_EXIT_DISC from a route. Ze exposes the full obligation as `modify NAME { del { med; } }` on an import chain, before Decision Process phases 1 and 2.

## Published support status

**Area:** BGP-4 base protocol

**Test verification:** 126 of 133 MUST proven

**Evidence:** interop 5, functional 13, unit 108

**Unmet:** RFC4271-5.1.6-1, RFC4271-6.2-3, RFC4271-9.1.2.2-2, RFC4271-9.2-2, RFC4271-9.2-3, RFC4271-9.2.1.1-2, RFC4271-9.2.1.1-3

The MUST bar shows mandatory requirements (MUST, MUST NOT, REQUIRED, SHALL and SHALL NOT) that Ze and its operating system must meet. It includes only RFCs whose requirement lists have been checked against the original text. Ze verified means a test has been shown to fail when the required behavior is deliberately broken. Behavior provided by the operating system or a library is OS verified when a test checks it using the settings Ze installs and has also been shown to detect a deliberate fault; until then, it is an OS testing gap. Tests awaiting verification counts requirements a tagged test covers that no deliberate fault has yet been shown to break and that nothing records as unmet. Exclusion unchecked counts requirements claimed not to apply to Ze whose evidence is still to be checked. Not assessed counts requirements no tagged test covers and that nothing records as unmet, so nothing yet says whether Ze meets them. Not built counts requirements the RFC summary or a test review says Ze does not meet, in full or in part. The overview bar adds optional requirements (MAY, NOT RECOMMENDED, OPTIONAL, RECOMMENDED, SHOULD and SHOULD NOT) and requirements that do not apply to Ze. An RFC marked requirements not reviewed is excluded from these figures because its requirement list has not been checked against the original text. Counts are loaded from the RFC data files.

Source: [data/rfc/rfc4271.json](../../../data/rfc/rfc4271.json).

A recorded admission bypass proves the negative raw-export /24-to-/25 check on ordinary and native route-server sockets with the answer marker retained or stripped: all six cases pass clean, then all four marked cases emit forbidden /25 output while both unmarked controls pass. These records do not prove MP NLRI, all native families, stored-route replay or prior-advertisement withdrawal. The restricted boundary is internal/component/bgp/reactor/filter_atomic_aggregate.go::atomicAggregatePolicyAllowed; the stronger product restriction is documented in docs/architecture/bgp/egress-attribute-rules.md. **Partial test coverage:** Tests and fault test records cover only the part marked Tested; they do not verify the whole requirement. partial (the tests enforce only the declared tested scope; the whole requirement remains unmet), fresh. Independent scoped rejudge after rereading RFC 4271 Sections 5.1.6 and 9.1.4, the current partial annotation, all three tagged units, their socket relay helpers, runLibraryChain and both producing boundaries. Section 5.1.6 binds the route as received with ATOMIC_AGGREGATE even when export policy strips the answer marker; Section 9.1.4 forbids deaggregation. Tested is "make any NLRI of that route more specific"; Gap is "advertising this route to other BGP speakers"; Producer is internal/component/bgp/reactor/filter_atomic_aggregate.go::atomicAggregatePolicyAllowed. The release restriction accepts only original native identities and a retained marker for a marked input that still announces; its equality rule is deliberately stronger than the RFC containment obligation. The legacy positive unit requires exact received /17 and /24 NLRI, zero-length ATOMIC_AGGREGATE and changed MED after an attribute-policy rebuild on ordinary and native route-server socket rails. The independent-specific control requires exact separately received unmarked /25 and /32 output on both rails, excluding a blanket prefix-length clamp. Their existing builder mutant covers remain distinct and do not by themselves prove an independently observed mutant failure on each relay rail. The added negative unit sends a valid received /24 through raw export-policy /25 replacement, with the output marker retained and stripped on each rail; its two unmarked controls require exact /25 output and no withdrawals, while its four marked cases forbid covered more-specific socket output and permit safe suppression or preservation. Direct read of job-atomic-rfc-rewrite-record-f3037485.log confirms all six clean cases pass, then all four marked cases fail on forbidden decoded /25 socket output under the recorded admission bypass while both unmarked controls still pass. Canonical discrimination retains these three distinct covers; this rejudge creates no new mutant observation. These tags and records establish only the declared legacy IPv4 scope. They do not prove MP NLRI, every native family, stored-route replay, prior-advertisement withdrawal or broad ownership/recovery ordering. Those advertising paths remain unmet or unproven for this whole requirement. Verdict stays partial and earns zero whole-requirement credit. |

These matrices do not bound every detected OPEN validation class; internal/component/bgp/reactor/session_handlers.go::handleOpen remains the gap-context for the full all-errors obligation. A nine-octet OPEN body is a message-header length error, not the Section 6.2 framing witness. **Partial test coverage:** Tests and fault test records cover only the part marked Tested; they do not verify the whole requirement. partial (the tests enforce only the declared tested scope; the whole requirement remains unmet), fresh. RFC 4271 Section 6.2 says: All errors detected while processing the OPEN message MUST be indicated by sending the NOTIFICATION message with the Error Code OPEN Message Error. Tested is "NOTIFICATION message with the Error Code OPEN Message Error"; Gap is "All errors detected"; Producer is internal/component/bgp/reactor/session_handlers.go::handleOpen. The ordinary and pending matrices present three standard/RFC9072 outer-length failures with legal message-header lengths. Both now reach Session.admitOpen, UnpackOpen and rejectOpenCapabilityError; they require exact NOTIFICATION 2/0, ordinary Idle or retained incumbent OpenConfirm, and cleared pending admission. The pending fixture first reads its independent connection's local OPEN before sending malformed input, preserving the refusal assertions. Exact-length ordinary controls require KEEPALIVE and OpenConfirm; the valid pending collision requires Cease 6/7 and an unchanged incumbent. These controls distinguish framing refusal from unconditional OPEN or collision refusal. The handleOpen extraction delegates decoding and validation without changing this tested obligation. The matrices do not enumerate every OPEN validation class, assert TCP EOF independently, or prove universal incoming admission; this remains partial with zero whole-requirement credit. |

| `RFC4271-5.1.2-3` | When a given BGP speaker advertises the route to an external peer, the advertising speaker updates the AS_PATH attribute as follows: 1) if the first path segment of the AS_PATH is of type AS_SEQUENCE, the local system prepends its own AS number as the last element of the sequence (put it in the leftmost position with respect to the position of octets in the protocol message). If the act of prepending will cause an overflow in the AS_PATH segment (i.e., more than 255 ASes), it SHOULD prepend a new segment of type AS_SEQUENCE and prepend its own AS number to this new segment. 2) if the first path segment of the AS_PATH is of type AS_SET, the local system prepends a new path segment of type AS_SEQUENCE to the AS_PATH, including its own AS number in that segment. 3) if the AS_PATH is empty, the local system creates a path segment of type AS_SEQUENCE, places its own AS into that segment, and places that segment into the AS_PATH. (§5.1.2) | SHALL | 5.1.2 | **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestASPathSlotPrependOnlyWhenAdvertising`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/bgp/wireu/rfc4271_advertise_test.go#L97). **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestASPathSlotPrependsBeforeALeadingASSet`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/bgp/wireu/rfc4271_aspath_slot_test.go#L358). **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestASPathSlotStartsANewSegmentWhenTheLeadingOneIsFull`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/bgp/wireu/rfc4271_aspath_slot_test.go#L392). **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestEstablishedAnnounce_ExplicitASPath_PrependsLocalAS`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/bgp/reactor/rfc4271_reactor_batch_test.go#L543). **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestForwardPresentEmptyASPath`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/bgp/reactor/rfc4271_aspath_empty_test.go#L23). **negative:** `unit/verify` [`TestASPathSlotPrependOnlyWhenAdvertising`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/bgp/wireu/rfc4271_advertise_test.go#L99). **negative:** `unit/verify` [`TestEstablishedAnnounce_ExplicitASPath_IBGPVerbatim`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/bgp/reactor/rfc4271_reactor_batch_test.go#L567). **negative:** `unit/verify` [`TestForwardWithdrawalDoesNotCreateASPath`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/bgp/reactor/rfc4271_withdraw_aspath_pair_test.go#L24). **positive:** `interop/nightly` [`checkRelayWithdrawalShape`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/le/interoplab/bgp/check_rfc.go#L473). **negative:** `interop/nightly` [`checkRelayWithdrawalShape`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/le/interoplab/bgp/check_rfc.go#L474) |
| `RFC4271-5.1.4-3` | If a BGP speaker is configured to alter the value of the MULTI_EXIT_DISC attribute received over EBGP, then altering the value MUST be done prior to determining the degree of preference of the route and prior to performing route selection (Decision Process phases 1 and 2). (§5.1.4) | MUST | 5.1.4 | **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestRFC4271MEDAlterationHappensAtIngress`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/bgp/reactor/rfc4271_test.go#L458). **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestRFC4271ReceivedMEDAlteredBeforeTheDecisionProcess`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/bgp/reactor/rfc4271_med_ingress_test.go#L42). **negative:** no negative test. **{single-polarity}:** the requirement constrains only the ORDER of an alteration that a speaker chooses to make, so there is no non-conformant input a receiver could reject. ze's only place to alter a received MULTI_EXIT_DISC is the ingress filter chain, whose rewritten payload replaces the WireUpdate before the UPDATE is dispatched to the RIB plugin that runs phases 1 and 2 (internal/component/bgp/reactor/reactor_notify.go:427-466) |

(§9.1.3) | SHALL NOT | 9.1.3 | **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestRFC4271OrdinaryOutputRequiresForwardableRoute`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/bgp/reactor/rfc4271_forwardability_session_test.go#L37). **negative:** `unit/verify` [`TestRFC4271OrdinaryOutputRequiresForwardableRoute`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/bgp/reactor/rfc4271_forwardability_session_test.go#L92). **negative:** `unit/verify` [`TestRFC4271OrdinaryReplacementRecipientSkipsUnresolvedRoute`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/bgp/reactor/rfc4271_forwardability_session_test.go#L167). **{partial}:** tested "may be forwarded appropriately by the Routing Table"; gap "installed in the Adj-Rib-Out"; the ordinary IPv4 internal-plugin witnesses check sampled destination and emitted NEXT_HOP resolvability at stable successful recipient writes, initial unresolved-route exclusion, reachability loss/recovery and replacement-recipient replay. The remaining stable successful-write oracle is unproven across forked prospective resolution in internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib_next_hop.go::remoteRIB.prospectiveDistance, Routing-Table revision invalidation in internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib_remote.go::RIBManager.runRemoteMetrics, and remote route publication. Earlier competing received-route replay advertised retained unresolved input to a replacement Session; the ordinary-replay-owned guard now stands that auxiliary replay down, and TestRFC4271OrdinaryReplacementRecipientSkipsUnresolvedRoute has a recorded structured-guard negative at its observed source version. Fresh native retakes observed the changed mirror omission producing successful unforwardable destination output and the structured replay-guard omission leaking retained unresolved input to a useful replacement recipient; the unchanged local prospective-admission negative remains a separate recorded cover. Independently reread both complete current roots, their framing/write/revision/history and settlement helpers, actual local and remote candidate/publication producers and structured replay guard, and the RFC's full Phase 3 context. TestRFC4271OrdinaryOutputRequiresForwardableRoute has distinct positive and negative tags. Its independently resolvable control must reach the actual ordinary internal recipient; initially unresolved retained input must yield zero announcements and no destination cover after ingress, plugin-event, forwarding and capture settlement. Reachability insertion, loss withdrawal and restoration then require useful recovery without another source UPDATE, preserving attributes and control output. The observer samples each subject/control destination and actual emitted NEXT_HOP under one stable Routing-Table revision around a complete successful transport write, independently pairing observations with captured announcement history. Framing errors, short writes, crossed revisions, lost history and bounded-observer overflow fail the fixture. This is neither a kernel-forwarding acknowledgment nor a blanket family/platform claim.

TestRFC4271OrdinaryReplacementRecipientSkipsUnresolvedRoute establishes useful original P/K, removes P's reachability, requires its service withdrawal while retained received input survives, closes and joins the original capture, then requires an actual distinct recipient Session and nonzero different destination token with the same selected owner. Useful K must reach the replacement while unresolved retained P has zero announcements; reachability restoration recovers P with no new source input. The structured ordinary-replay guard is now a tested auxiliary-replay exclusion boundary, not the remaining remote gap.

| `RFC4271-9.1.2-5` | If the AS_PATH attribute of a BGP route contains an AS loop, the BGP route should be excluded from the Phase 2 decision function (§9.1.2). Detection scans the full AS path and checks that the local autonomous system number does not appear in it. RFC 4271 writes this keyword in lower case, so the level is a recommendation and not a capitalized RFC 2119 SHOULD. The same paragraph places a speaker configured to accept routes with its own autonomous system number in the AS path outside the scope of the document. That out-of-scope case is what the allow-own-as setting selects, so a non-zero allow-own-as is not a deviation from this line. | SHOULD | 9.1.2 | **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestDetectASLoop_NotPresent`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/bgp/reactor/loopdetect/rfc4271_loop_test.go#L136). **negative:** `unit/verify` [`TestDetectASLoop`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/bgp/reactor/loopdetect/rfc4271_loop_test.go#L114). **negative:** `unit/verify` [`TestDetectASLoop_ASSet`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/bgp/reactor/loopdetect/rfc4271_loop_test.go#L125). **negative:** `functional/verify` [`loop-as.ci`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/test/plugin/loop-as.ci#L7) |

## Gaps and untested MUSTs

| Requirement | State | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| [`RFC4271-5.1.6-1`](#rfc4271-5.1.6-1) A BGP speaker that receives a route with the ATOMIC_AGGREGATE attribute MUST NOT make any NLRI of that route more specific (as defined in 9.1.4) when advertising this route to other BGP speakers. (§5.1.6) | {partial}, Partial test coverage | tested "make any NLRI of that route more specific"; gap "advertising this route to other BGP speakers"; legacy IPv4 relay controls preserve exact NLRI during attribute rebuilds and retain independently received more-specific routes. A recorded admission bypass proves the negative raw-export /24-to-/25 check on ordinary and native route-server sockets with the answer marker retained or stripped: all six cases pass clean, then all four marked cases emit forbidden /25 output while both unmarked controls pass. These records do not prove MP NLRI, all native families, stored-route replay or prior-advertisement withdrawal. The restricted boundary is internal/component/bgp/reactor/filter_atomic_aggregate.go::atomicAggregatePolicyAllowed; the stronger product restriction is documented in docs/architecture/bgp/egress-attribute-rules.md |
| [`RFC4271-6.2-3`](#rfc4271-6.2-3) All errors detected while processing the OPEN message MUST be indicated by sending the NOTIFICATION message with the Error Code OPEN Message Error. (§6.2) | {partial}, Partial test coverage | tested "NOTIFICATION message with the Error Code OPEN Message Error"; gap "All errors detected"; standard and RFC 9072 outer OPEN framing failures now produce exact NOTIFICATION 2/0 through the ordinary decoder and pending-connection reader; exact-length OPEN controls advance the ordinary handshake, while a valid pending collision retains Cease 6/7. These matrices do not bound every detected OPEN validation class; internal/component/bgp/reactor/session_handlers.go::handleOpen remains the gap-context for the full all-errors obligation. A nine-octet OPEN body is a message-header length error, not the Section 6.2 framing witness |
| [`RFC4271-9.1.2.2-2`](#rfc4271-9.1.2.2-2) If an implementation chooses to remove MULTI_EXIT_DISC, then the optional comparison on MULTI_EXIT_DISC, if performed, MUST be performed only among EBGP-learned routes. (§9.1.2.2) | {gap}, no test | the common egress guard is implemented, but normal BGP selected-route readvertisement has no runnable producer and no discriminating proof. `bgp-rib` records selected Loc-RIB state (internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib_bestchange.go:738-790), route-server and route-reflector plugins forward cached UPDATEs instead (internal/component/bgp/plugins/rs/server.go:433-434 and internal/component/bgp/plugins/rr/rr.go:188-195), and BGP-to-BGP redistribution is rejected as same-protocol redistribution (internal/core/redistevents/registry.go:144-146) |
| [`RFC4271-9.2-2`](#rfc4271-9.2-2) A route SHALL NOT be installed in the Adj-Rib-Out unless the destination, and NEXT_HOP described by this route, may be forwarded appropriately by the Routing Table. (§9.1.3) | {partial}, Partial test coverage | tested "may be forwarded appropriately by the Routing Table"; gap "installed in the Adj-Rib-Out"; the ordinary IPv4 internal-plugin witnesses check sampled destination and emitted NEXT_HOP resolvability at stable successful recipient writes, initial unresolved-route exclusion, reachability loss/recovery and replacement-recipient replay. The remaining stable successful-write oracle is unproven across forked prospective resolution in internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib_next_hop.go::remoteRIB.prospectiveDistance, Routing-Table revision invalidation in internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib_remote.go::RIBManager.runRemoteMetrics, and remote route publication. Earlier competing received-route replay advertised retained unresolved input to a replacement Session; the ordinary-replay-owned guard now stands that auxiliary replay down, and TestRFC4271OrdinaryReplacementRecipientSkipsUnresolvedRoute has a recorded structured-guard negative at its observed source version. Fresh native retakes observed the changed mirror omission producing successful unforwardable destination output and the structured replay-guard omission leaking retained unresolved input to a useful replacement recipient; the unchanged local prospective-admission negative remains a separate recorded cover. These are proof limits, not demonstrated absence of the remote behavior. Section 9.1.3's explicit local-policy exception remains supported and is not exercised here. |
| [`RFC4271-9.2-3`](#rfc4271-9.2-3) If a route in Loc-RIB is excluded from a particular Adj-RIB-Out, the previously advertised route in that Adj-RIB-Out MUST be withdrawn from service by means of an UPDATE message (§9.1.3) | {partial}, Partial test coverage | tested "previously advertised route"; gap "a route in Loc-RIB is excluded from a particular Adj-RIB-Out"; live general and route-server relays withdraw the exact previously advertised IPv4 prefix after export rejection of a newer received generation; accepted-generation controls carry changed MED without withdrawal. Their shared-chain mutation can stop at the first general-rail failure, so it is not a separate observed route-server mutant red. The ordinary internal-plugin IPv4 selected witness also proves the higher-LOCAL_PREF global winner is denied, its previous lower-path advertisement is withdrawn once, both received candidates remain stored, and a useful marker reaches the same recipient without lower-candidate promotion. A fresh native cut suppressing only selected IPv4 withdrawal bytes in Session.writeUpdateBody made that useful witness fail at its required recipient withdrawal; the earlier retirement cut stayed green and supplied no proof. The remaining gap includes equivalent service-withdrawal proof across the framed forked selected publication boundary in pkg/plugin/sdk/sdk_selected.go::Plugin.SelectRoutes, mixed-origin inputs, and refresh or stale replay. These are proof limits, not demonstrated absence of those implementations; this partial row gives no whole-requirement credit. |
| [`RFC4271-9.2.1.1-2`](#rfc4271-9.2.1.1-2) Two UPDATE messages sent by a BGP speaker to a peer that advertise feasible routes and/or withdrawal of unfeasible routes to some common set of destinations MUST be separated by at least MinRouteAdvertisementIntervalTimer. (§9.2.1.1) | {gap}, no test | ze has no MinRouteAdvertisementIntervalTimer, so successive UPDATEs to a common set of destinations are not spaced. The timer set implements only ConnectRetry, Hold and Keepalive and records the omission in its own doc comment (internal/component/bgp/fsm/timer.go:34-42, "MinRouteAdvertisementIntervalTimer (Section 9.2.1.1) - not implemented here"); `grep -rniE 'minroute\|mrai' --include=*.go internal/` finds no producer |
| [`RFC4271-9.2.1.1-3`](#rfc4271-9.2.1.1-3) If new routes are selected multiple times while awaiting the expiration of MinRouteAdvertisementIntervalTimer, the last route selected SHALL be advertised at the end of MinRouteAdvertisementIntervalTimer. (§9.2.1.1) | {gap}, no test | with no MinRouteAdvertisementIntervalTimer there is no expiry at which a last-selected route could be advertised. The timer is absent by design note (internal/component/bgp/fsm/timer.go:39) and no producer buffers a pending best-route advertisement against such a timer; best-path changes are published as they are computed (internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib_bestchange.go:832-880) |

## Test status

A test reads not verified when no record shows it failing after a deliberate fault. Until then, the test has not been shown to detect a fault in the required behavior.

### [`RFC4271-4.1-1`](#rfc4271-4.1-1)

This 16-octet field is included for compatibility; it MUST be set to all ones. (§4.1)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Sender side. TestRFC4271EveryMessageSentWithAllOnesMarker packs OPEN (classic, extended, >255), UPDATE, NOTIFICATION, KEEPALIVE, ROUTE-REFRESH into a fresh buffer and at offset 7 of a zero-filled buffer and asserts the 16 marker octets equal 0xFF each time; red if writeHeader writes any other marker. Receive-side (6.1) tags kept.

### [`RFC4271-4.1-3`](#rfc4271-4.1-3)

The value of the Length field MUST always be at least 19 and no greater than 4096 (§4.1)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. RFC 4271 Section 4.1: “The value of the Length field MUST always be at least 19 and no greater than 4096, and MAY be further constrained, depending on the message type.” The changed sender test supplies an equivalent all-false per-family ADD-PATH selector. It establishes that 1500 prefixes exceed one 4096-byte UPDATE, encodes every emitted chunk, requires declared Length equal to written octets within 19..4096, and requires every prefix in original order. Receive controls accept 19, 1000 and 4096 and reject 18, 4097 and 65535 without Extended Message, with exact header-error classification. Producer tracing confirms size budgets include the BGP header; the final ordinary writer sizes effective post-policy bytes, consumes split chunks synchronously and refuses unencodable output instead of emitting a truncated frame. Negotiated RFC 8654 extension remains a separate qualification. This judgment is based on assertions and current source, not producer-halt proof alone.

### [`RFC4271-4.4-1`](#rfc4271-4.4-1)

KEEPALIVE messages MUST NOT be sent more frequently than one per second (§4.4)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Independent re-judgment after 562bc0139d against RFC 4271 Section 4.4 (KEEPALIVE messages MUST NOT be sent more frequently than one per second). writeMessageHeld's new step applies to *message.Notification only; a KEEPALIVE passes unchanged. publishNegotiation still raises a negotiated hold of 1-2 s to 3 s and replaces a configured keepalive at or above the negotiated hold with hold/3; the configured keepalive is whole seconds (reactor/config.go), so no configured interval is sub-second. The tests are unchanged: the positive pins the first and the periodic fire at exactly 1 s with zero jitter at hold 3; the negative checks that hold 3, 30 and 90 fire at hold/3 and never inside the first second, and that hold 0 sends none. The one-shot KEEPALIVEs on OPEN receipt and on BFD strict-mode release (session_bfd_strict.go) are one per transition. Limit of the proof: the tests drive the fsm timer, not publishNegotiation's clamp; a hold of 1 or 2 from the peer is refused before negotiation by the RFC4271-4.2-1 validator negative, not by these tests.

### [`RFC4271-4.4-2`](#rfc4271-4.4-2)

If the negotiated Hold Time interval is zero, then periodic KEEPALIVE messages MUST NOT be sent. (§4.4)

Negative: negotiated zero from either side -> timers hold 0, KeepaliveTimer stopped, zero octets in 1.6 s. ### [`RFC4271-5-1`](#rfc4271-5-1)

BGP implementations MUST recognize all well-known attributes (§5)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Independent final first source judgment of RFC4271-5-1 against full Section5 and Section4.3 attribute definitions, updated RFC7606 error handling, current complete tags and actual registration/validation paths. The positive complete legal iBGP/ASN4 bundle contains all five true well-known codes ORIGIN/AS_PATH/NEXT_HOP/LOCAL_PREF/ATOMIC_AGGREGATE, plus optional MED/AGGREGATOR. It requires exact ActionNone. Source registration declares all five WellKnownFlags, init calls RegisterName to mark recognition, and the real validator refuses any missing well-known recognition bit before per-value validation. Removing any of those five recognition bits therefore cannot pass this positive unchanged. LOCAL_PREF misrecognition is a genuine recognition violation, not allowed route-policy rejection; the pure validator has no such policy callback. Explicit attribute registrations/options remain supported.
The negative supplies six per-code malformed lengths: ORIGIN,NEXT_HOP,MED,LOCAL_PREF,ATOMIC_AGGREGATE,AGGREGATOR; AS_PATH has no malformed leaf here. It requires non-None action and matching AttrCode for these cases, then exact TAW/code1 for Optional-ORIGIN and Transitive-clear-ORIGIN, and exact TAW/code1 for missingORIGIN. These supplementary malformed/flags/missing controls defeat an unconditional-None implementation. They do NOT prove exact revised handling for every length: None-versus-error allows stronger reset, and optional extras are not well-known obligations. In particular an unrecognized LOCAL_PREF reset still carries AttrCode5 and would pass its malformed-length leaf. The applied independently judged comment repair now explicitly calls these supplementary listed-malformation/ORIGIN-flags controls, states that the length checks admit same-code unknown-attribute reset, and disclaims malformed AS_PATH or independent per-code recognition. The positive now identifies the five-code recognition guard and optional extras without claiming per-code invocation tracing. Every original input/assertion and tagID/polarity/order remains unchanged. This resolves the identified prose overclaims without inventing an exact-action or exhaustive-dispatch oracle.
The whole baseline recognition obligation has a genuine positive witness via the shared recognized-bit guard plus complete legal bundle, with source registration binding all five; the negative is a limited companion, not independent per-code recognition authority. Independent source rejudgment over the corrected applied postimage preserves enforced recognition scope. Claim fidelity is resolved by the applied independently judged comment repair; actual paired receipts and scoped native freshness are reported below. The applied change qualifies comments only, with no assertion/input or obligation reduction; this judge authored none of those test changes. Preserve all existing assertions/features and RFC7606's modern TAW/attribute-discard behavior.

The corrected test faithfully identifies its complete five-well-known-code recognition bundle while treating MED/AGGREGATOR as optional context, without claiming codec invocation tracing or exhaustive per-code semantic mutants.

### [`RFC4271-5-2`](#rfc4271-5-2)

Some of these attributes are mandatory and MUST be included in every UPDATE message that contains NLRI. (§5)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), stale-unit: internal/core/bgp/attribute/origin.go moved. Independent final companion whole-row source-and-actual-receipt rejudgment of RFC4271-5-2 over the actual approved claim postimage: enforced. Both changed receiver receipts and actual native all-cover binding freshness are read below; final stamping remains main-owned. Full RFC4271 Section5 says "Some of these attributes are mandatory and MUST be included in every UPDATE message that contains NLRI." Section4.3 identifies ORIGIN, AS_PATH and legacy NEXT_HOP as well-known mandatory; RFC7606 Section3(d) says missing well-known mandatory attributes use treat-as-withdraw and notes RFC4760 reclassifies NEXT_HOP for MP use. The Tx inclusion MUST is not rewritten into a universal duty to accept a received bundle. META assesses the normal configuration path, preserves explicit operator extensions and policy, and does not require literal FSM objects. The unchanged quote, level, all five tagged covers and every body/assertion/input remain in scope.
The genuine transmitting witness is TestRFC4271UpdateWithNLRICarriesTheMandatoryAttributes, carrying separate stated-route positive and bare-route negative tags. For each route it runs real NewUpdateBuilder65001 with eBGP/iBGP, ASN4true and AddPathfalse; usable IPv4 next hop, stated ORIGINIGP/path65002,65003 versus omitted Origin/ASPath, and independent prefixes. mustBuildUnicast fails on a build error, bodyNLRI must be nonempty, and requireMandatoryWithNLRI walks actual encoded PathAttributes with attrCodesInBlob and requires codes1/2/3. BuildUnicast unconditionally appends typed Origin (zero isIGP), appendASPath always emits an AS_PATH wrapper even for empty iBGP path or eBGP self-path, and native IPv4 next hop appends NEXT_HOP. Real ordered attribute encoding writes header/value bytes into the returned scratch-backed Update. The oracle checks encoded attribute presence, not every value's correctness, whole-message packing/socket delivery or all routes/features as individually observed cases; the source supplies the applicable emission path and shared packer. Its bare-input negative is a caller-omission contrast requiring mandatory defaults, not an emitted malformed wire carrier. No configured feature is removed to obtain those defaults.
The same builder root also retains its existing explicit Extended Next Hop IPv6 case: bodyNLRI must be empty and MP_REACH present on eBGP/iBGP. That preserves the applicable8950/4760 representation rather than demanding a legacy NEXT_HOP for MP-carried NLRI. Separate tagged TestBuildUnicastRefusesIPv4RouteWithNoUsableNextHop has two children, IPv6 next hop without Extended Next Hop and unset next hop. It requires errors.Is ErrUnicastNextHopUnusable and nil Update. checkUnicastNextHop runs before scratch/attribute construction; unusable legacy inputs cannot escape with bodyNLRI but noNEXT_HOP. This is a meaningful builder refusal negative, not a blanket prohibition on the preserved negotiated extended form. Untagged extended-flag/IPv4-next-hop control was read as context only; it is not silently added as a new tagged runtime cover.
The two receive covers are distinct, explicitly qualified companions. It establishes this current receive contract/recognition bundle, not independently tracing optional codecs or proving the transmitter inclusion MUST. A policy rejection outside this pure classifier is not prohibited by this test claim; a present-ORIGIN-forgotten fault is a software classification contrast and supplies no sole mandatory Txcredit. The genuine builder evidence above remains separate.

### [`RFC4271-5-3`](#rfc4271-5-3)

If a path with an unrecognized transitive optional attribute is accepted and passed to other BGP peers, then the unrecognized transitive optional attribute of that path MUST be passed, along with the path, to other BGP peers with the Partial bit in the Attribute Flags octet set to 1. (§5)

Audit verdict: weak (the tests pass over code that does not enforce the requirement), stale-unit: internal/component/bgp/reactor/reactor_api_forward.go::reactorAPIAdapter.forwardUpdateSection moved. Independent re-judgment after adding emitPrePolicyAdjRIBOut in reactorAPIAdapter.forwardUpdateSection (reactor_api_forward.go) for RFC 8671 Section 5.2 and unresolvedNextHops withdrawal cleanup in RIBManager.handleReceivedStructured (rib_structured.go, commit 04c75f30c3). Neither change touches attribute parsing, unrecognized transitive optional attribute retention, or Partial bit setting/preservation: emitPrePolicyAdjRIBOut copies sourceWire.Payload() read-only for BMP pre-policy Adj-RIB-Out observation without mutating sourceWire, mods, or the transmitted UPDATE payload; handleReceivedStructured only purges unresolvedNextHops when a peer's RIB empties on withdrawal. All six tagged tests and their positive covers remain unchanged, and the verdict stays weak for the same recorded reason: every tag on RFC4271-5-3 is positive, with neither a negative test nor a {single-polarity} annotation.

The other units on the stale list changed in other commits (writeUpdateBody in 735771cf03, negotiated family admission on raw UPDATE writes; rib_recovery, rib_structured, storage/routeentry.go) and add no negative. The verdict stays weak for its recorded reason: every tag on RFC4271-5-4 is positive, the row has neither a negative test nor a {single-polarity} annotation (./le rfc check reports "has no negative test"), so for this MUST NOT no test feeds the violating case (a recognized transitive optional attribute received with Partial 1) and observes Ze refusing to clear it as distinct from a path that never clears any flag. The positive covers listed in the tests map remain as the prior verdict describes them.

### [`RFC4271-4.3-5`](#rfc4271-4.3-5)

However, a BGP speaker MUST be able to process UPDATE messages in this form. (§4.3)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), stale-unit: internal/component/bgp/reactor/rfc7947_session_first_as_test.go::firstASReceive, internal/component/bgp/reactor/session.go::NewSession, internal/component/bgp/reactor/session_prefix.go::Session.checkPrefixLimits, internal/component/bgp/reactor/session_read.go moved. Independent final source-and-receipt rejudgment of RFC4271-4.3-5: enforced. Full RFC4271 Section4.3 says, "However, a BGP speaker MUST be able to process UPDATE messages in this form." The preceding sentence names the same prefix in WITHDRAWN ROUTES and NLRI; the following announce-wins treatment is separately SHOULD4.3-7. RFC7606 Section5.1 retains the receiver MUST be prepared for these fields in any position or combination despite its newer sender restrictions. The current single-polarity-positive annotation isolates mandatory receiver support and explicitly leaves the RIB winner unrestricted. META default-baseline compliance and permitted explicit operator extensions are preserved; no literal FSM object or mandatory internal representation is inferred.
The complete current tagged inventory is the new TestSessionRFC4271SamePrefixWithdrawAndAnnounceIsProcessed only. The old three RIB tests retain their assertions under4.3-7 and are no longer4.3-5 tags. Their old Len1/announce-wins cuts were withdrawn as MUST discrimination and do not support this verdict. The new test uses an otherwise unfiltered established legacy eBGP Session, valid ORIGIN0, two-octet AS_SEQUENCE65002 matching the peer, valid NEXT_HOP, and exact independently framed203.0.113.0/24 in both legacy sections. firstASSession exchanges real OPEN/KEEPALIVE over net.Pipe, negotiates the explicit peer HoldTime0 to isolate receive processing, and registers the negotiated receive context. This is a receive-processing fixture, not a claim that every handshake setting remains default.
firstASReceive observes the actual semantic consumer callback, not the raw observer. It requires successful ReadAndProcess, successful peer write, Established state and exactly one received UPDATE Snapshot. Initial and following ordinary children additionally require exact NLRI; the mixed child does not require unchanged body/fields, a stored route, or any final winner. A conforming normalizer can omit the duplicate withdrawal. Session.processMessage classifies the valid input; processValidatedMessage applies RFC7606/AS-path processing and prefix admission before deliverReceivedMessage invokes the semantic callback. Snapshot owns the callback payload. Thus a successful return that silently discards this specific supported carrier before its sole consumer handoff cannot pass merely by avoiding an error or retaining Established state.
Actual receipt tmp/session/2026-11-15-153337/scratch/job-rfc4271-mixed-update-acceptance-proof-65261a74.log names the clean root and all three children PASS and an accepted current positive discrimination record. Its selective normal-return processValidatedMessage fault drops only UPDATEs whose successfully parsed, nonempty legacy withdrawal and NLRI bytes are equal; all ordinary handling is retained. The broken initial_ordinary and following_ordinary children both PASS. The mixed same_prefix child fails exactly at caller41 with "received 0 semantic UPDATEs, want one". The helper reached that check after successful ReadAndProcess, peer write and Established checks; no invented timeout, parse/reset, panic, callback-field or RIB-winner failure is counted. Following ordinary is an independently observed later child, not a fatal-skipped control.
The accepted canonical RFC4271-4.3-5 discrimination file contains exactly this new Session positive tuple and the real processValidatedMessage producer. Each source claim is limited to this valid unfiltered legacy same-prefix receive carrier and semantic handoff, with ordinary controls; this is not foreign-daemon, arbitrary-capability, downstream RIB or kernel evidence. The older canonical audit described now-retagged RIB units and must be replaced through mode rejudge, not mechanically resealed. This judgment repairs the former proof-isolation gap using actual new source and accepted receipt. Native current-record freshness, complete tag binding and final audit stamping remain main-owned and have not been executed here. Empty code values are intentional; no manual fingerprints or cover keys are authored.

### [`RFC4271-5.1.3-1`](#rfc4271-5.1.3-1)

A route originated by a BGP speaker SHALL NOT be advertised to a peer using an address of that peer as NEXT_HOP. (§5.1.3)

Audit verdict: weak (the tests pass over code that does not enforce the requirement), stale-unit: internal/component/bgp/reactor/rfc4271_forward_next_hop_test.go::TestEgressNextHopIsPeerOwnReadsTheRewrittenAddress, internal/component/bgp/reactor/rfc4271_forward_next_hop_test.go::TestEgressNextHopIsPeerOwnReadsTheRewrittenAddress#2, internal/component/bgp/reactor/rfc4271_forward_next_hop_test.go::TestSendUpdateWithholdsOriginatedRouteWithPeerOwnNextHop, internal/component/bgp/reactor/rfc4271_forward_next_hop_test.go::TestSendUpdateWithholdsOriginatedRouteWithPeerOwnNextHop#2, internal/component/bgp/reactor/rfc4271_scoped_next_hop_test.go::TestRFC4271ScopedNextHopPreservesThirdParty, internal/component/bgp/reactor/rfc4271_scoped_next_hop_test.go::TestRFC4271ScopedNextHopWithholdsPeerOwn, internal/le/interoplab/bgp/check_rfc.go::checkSelfNextHopWithheld, internal/le/interoplab/bgp/check_rfc.go::checkSelfNextHopWithheld#2, test/plugin/originated-nexthop-peer-own.ci, test/plugin/originated-nexthop-peer-own.ci#2, test/plugin/raw-originated-nexthop-peer-own.ci, test/plugin/raw-originated-nexthop-peer-own.ci#2 moved. Independent current-source rejudgment of the whole RFC4271 Section 5.1.3 sentence: A route originated by a BGP speaker SHALL NOT be advertised to a peer using an address of that peer as NEXT_HOP. The prior unimplemented finding described a real raw-originated leak, but its raw producer description is obsolete. Current writeOriginatedRawUpdate asks aigpNextHop(body).has(s.settings.Address); writeOriginatedPeerOwnUpdate refuses the offending active announcement while splitting mixed input to preserve original withdrawals and permitted families. Its retained input protects synchronous callbacks that reuse the session write buffer. The registered diagnostic identifies the refusal and corrective action. Q1: the raw unit's exact absence of prohibited NLRI and literal useful prefix plus third-party NEXT_HOP, mixed-family and borrowed-input controls, ordinary typed writer, queued-default, effective-rewrite, relay, functional text-update and FRR decode oracles judge the advertising prohibition. Q2: main observed the original raw leak RED, then the primary raw unit, mixed-family unit and generic borrowed-input unit PASS under race in job-next-hop-raw-guard-green-239822e1.log; all named subcases passed. The actual raw daemon draft also passed both command forms with same-session useful preceding/following announcements, literal third-party NEXT_HOP and owed withdrawals in job-next-hop-raw-daemon-green-a4275b22.log. These are scoped observations, not current selective mutant records, promoted functional evidence or completed FRR acceptance. Q3: the new raw unit uses matching actual ASN4/family negotiation, a valid local AS path and explicit accepting export final; its third-party twin changes only NEXT_HOP. The existing three general/native relay tests still violate their post-ingress fixture boundary: nhForward and nhForwardRS put nhPayload's ASN2 bytes/context into a cache that requires canonical four-octet AS-bearing input. TranscodeASPath only narrows and rejects this synthetic widening. Their owner-withdrawal assertions therefore fail for the wrong boundary condition; the canonical-byte fixture proposal remains pending. Q4: one unconditional prohibition covers typed and raw originated routes; no truthful source-clause partial partition exists. The identified raw admission behavior is now implemented and has scoped red-to-green evidence, so retaining its old source-gap annotation would be false. Whole-row enforcement remains unproven while the current relay fixture defects, current selective guard proofs, daemon promotion/stress, remaining raw controls and interop validation are outstanding. Verdict weak describes the currently confounded tagged population, retains existing meaningful typed/relay and new raw evidence, and claims no whole-requirement credit. No new current-source RFC4271-5.1.3-1 product defect is asserted by this disposition.

### [`RFC4271-5.1.3-3`](#rfc4271-5.1.3-3)

A BGP speaker MUST be able to support the disabling advertisement of third party NEXT_HOP attributes in order to handle imperfectly bridged media. (§5.1.3)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Re-judged 2026-10-01 (BGP c17 judge, residuals of the c16 verdict). Positive: TestRFC4271NextHopSelfWithAutoLocalAddressSendsTheConnectedEndpoint (next-hop self under `local ip auto`, session endpoint 10.0.0.77: on the general AND route-server forward rails the next-hop-self destination is sent NEXT_HOP 10.0.0.77, a default internal destination the third-party 192.0.2.254; resolveNextHop answers the same). HEAD TestRFC4271NextHopSelfDisablesThirdPartyNextHopOnTheWire keeps the configured-address wire case. Negative: TestRFC4271NextHopSelfWithNoLocalAddressWithholdsTheRoute and TestRFC4271ThirdPartyNextHopDisableFailsClosed (withhold with withdrawal still sent, nhSelfWithheld, ErrNextHopSelfNoLocal). Records on connectedLocalAddress (+), precomputeNextHop (- two units) observed red. c16 residuals closed in c17: (1) the WARN line is now asserted by untagged TestRFC4271NextHopSelfWithheldRouteIsLoggedAtWarn (Warn-level handler, both rails, exactly one line per withhold naming peer= and rfc="RFC 4271 Section 5.1.3"); (2) TestRFC4271ThirdPartyNextHopCanBeDisabled now carries a record on precomputeNextHop; (3) a link-local connected endpoint is now gated on both forward rails by the same predicate as the announce rail (Peer.linkLocalOnlyNextHopRefused via egressNextHopLinkLocalOnlyRefused), proven by untagged TestNextHopSelfLinkLocalEndpointNeedsTheCapabilityOnTheForwardRails; the judge's overlay disabling only the route-server gate reddens its rs=true case (j17-ov-rs.log). Quote verbatim (RFC 4271 Section 5.1.3).

### [`RFC4271-5.1.4-1`](#rfc4271-5.1.4-1)

The MULTI_EXIT_DISC attribute received from a neighboring AS MUST NOT be propagated to other neighboring ASes. (§5.1.4)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Independent source rejudgment of RFC 4271 Section 5.1.4: "The MULTI_EXIT_DISC attribute received from a neighboring AS MUST NOT be propagated to other neighboring ASes." Read all tagged Go units, both functional files, native checker and relevant producer/fixture. applyFactsMED strips a received unchanged metric for ordinary eBGP, but retains iBGP/route-server-client propagation and locally created or policy-replaced metrics. Go positives compare exact rebuilt attributes for metrics zero/100/max; controls retain the distinct allowed provenance. med-not-propagated-across-as compares full frames toward the RS client and ordinary external peer. The moved med-locally-set-reaches-peer still expects an exact MED=100 frame and the external fixture originates it through UpdateRoute; its accepting policy does not manufacture MED. This control's source/header naming should not be inflated to every established-session rail: the prior audit located it on queued initial replay, not writeAnnounceUpdate. checkMEDAcrossAS independently requires genuine source AS/NEXT_HOP blocks plus absent received MED and present locally originated MED=42. Verdict enforced with those precise controls, not a blanket ban on attribute 4. No execution or discrimination replay was performed.

No option, input, expect or requirement-tag line moved, so the .ci still requires the Adj-RIB-In stored route to carry no MED with the exact ORIGIN, AS_PATH and COMMUNITIES survivors. The other carriers (TestHandleFilterUpdateMEDRemoveIsImportOnly, med-removal-configured.ci, med-removal-export-refused.ci) are unchanged. Verdict unchanged: enforced.

### [`RFC4271-5.1.5-2`](#rfc4271-5.1.5-2)

A BGP speaker MUST NOT include this attribute in UPDATE messages it sends to external peers, except in the case of BGP Confederations [RFC3065]. (§5.1.5)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Independent scoped rejudgment on 2026-11-11. Read RFC 4271 section 5.1.5, short and derived rows, prior verdict, every currently tagged unit including new TestLocalPrefExternalWireViolationRefused, egress producers, current checker and discrimination records. RFC: A BGP speaker MUST NOT include this attribute in UPDATE messages it sends to external peers, except in the case of BGP Confederations [RFC3065]. Q1: assertions target emitted code 5, not the adjacent receiver-ignore obligation. TestRFC4271LocalPrefOmittedForExternalPeers checks absence while mandatory attributes survive. TestAnnounceStripsLocalPrefTowardExternalPeer covers packed and Builder inputs, queued/batch equality, and retained internal value. TestForwardLocalPrefStrippedToExternalPeer checks exact rebuilt bytes, while TestForwardLocalPrefStripBeatsAFilterSet covers filter-created zero. The functional file expects the complete external UPDATE. checkLocalPrefStrip reads the original external connection rather than normalized receiver RIB. Q2: removing suppression exposes an attribute these assertions reject; existing positive mutant record removes applyFactsLocalPref's Suppress and names assertion 13. The buildRIBRouteUpdate panic record only proves dependence on that function. New negative record targets requireLocalPrefInterval's final semantic-error return, replacing it with nil after complete structural validation; the native recorder first observed all three clean cases pass, then both leaked200/leaked0 cases fail under its per-run overlay while the stripped control still passed. The checkout source remained unchanged. The independent hand-written-break judge approved exactly that semantic break. Q3: source attributes and other output attributes remain controlled, all three arrivals and original session identity/fence/reverse KEEPALIVE are required. The new negative test holds normalized RIB answers and framing fixed, requires one release and both direction reads, and checks assertion 13 plus the exact wrapped localPrefWireSemanticError; setup or framing refusal cannot satisfy it. Q4: positive units cover originating and forwarding, source and filter supplied values including zero. localPrefAllowedTo gates batch, stored-RIB and wire builders; applyFactsLocalPref runs after filtering in both general and RS forwarding. Confederation configuration is absent, so the RFC exception has no reachable configured case. Polarity: internal-peer predicate/inclusion units and the functional source-arrival tag remain useful controls but are not relied on as violating inputs; the newly enrolled negative unit supplies forbidden external-wire LOCAL_PREF and requires its precise rejection, resolving the earlier independent polarity finding. checkLocalPrefStrip's later change 684b9f41f9 belongs to tooling-one-off-defects and adds assertion 16 to wait for Ze to hold both destinations Established before injector release, preserving prior checks. Carrier repair correctly labels the new Go test unit/verify rather than interop; its proof is checker-boundary evidence, not a new live daemon exchange. This auditor executed no tests, builds, lint, reports, stamp or replay; main owns the observed runs and native discrimination record. Verdict enforced on the reviewed population; no claim of additional live exchange.

### [`RFC4271-5.1.5-3`](#rfc4271-5.1.5-3)

If it is contained in an UPDATE message that is received from an external peer, then this attribute MUST be ignored by the receiving speaker, except in the case of BGP Confederations [RFC3065]. (§5.1.5)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Re-judged 2026-10-12 (independent auditor). SHIFTED by 4e731b2d96 (owner-approved lint-only reactor test edits). The tagged unit is byte-identical (gate-verified); the file-level changes are comment punctuation, slices.Concat over the same bytes, joined appends, strings.SplitSeq, newDefaultOriginatePeer taking its fixed addresses from constants with the same values, and registerTypeLenRecognizer losing a lo every caller passed as 1, none of which changes what a helper this unit calls produces. Verdict stands. RFC 4271 §5.1.5, rfc/full/rfc4271.txt:1599-1604: 'If it is contained in an UPDATE message that is received from an external peer, then this attribute MUST be ignored by the receiving speaker, except in the case of BGP Confederations [RFC3065].' The current full short row has this same receive-side sentence and no partial annotation. Read all four covers: internal/component/bgp/message/rfc4271_test.go::TestRFC4271LocalPrefKeptOnInternalSession requires ActionNone and empty DiscardEntries; ::TestRFC4271LocalPrefIgnoredOnExternalSession requires exactly AttributeDiscard, one entry, code 5; both polarities of internal/component/bgp/reactor/rfc_receive_session_dispatch_test.go::TestRFC4271LocalPrefFromExternalPeerNeverReachesTheRIB drive an actual Established session. The internal case requires byte-identical attributes including preference 200 and unchanged NLRI. The external case uses the matching four-octet peer AS, requires one dispatched UPDATE, exact attribute codes [1,2,3,252], absence of code 5, byte-identical retained ORIGIN/AS_PATH/NEXT_HOP, unchanged NLRI, Established state and no NOTIFICATION. The 252 marker is not mistaken for a surviving LOCAL_PREF.
Producing/consuming path read: message/rfc7606.go::validateLocalPrefAttr → reactor/session_validation.go::enforceRFC7606 → ApplyAttrDiscard/rebuild → session_read.go::processMessage → reactor_notify.go::notifyMessageReceiver. The latter derives RawBytes and AttrsWire from the rewritten WireUpdate, not the original raw body. rib_structured.go::handleReceivedStructured consumes that WireUpdate and packed attributes. Q1 yes: exact ignore-versus-retain behavior; Q2 yes by inspection: preserving external preference, dropping the UPDATE or stripping the internal control breaks explicit assertions; Q3 yes: valid established peers and matching external AS isolate preference handling; Q4 yes for the implemented non-confederation receive boundary, not send-side omission or entire RFC support. No confederation feature support or fresh semantic execution is inferred.
Pending note: use the preceding RFC quote, population, assertions and producer chain; retain enforced and state explicitly that this audit ran no tests and independent peer acceptance remains pending.

### [`RFC4271-5.1.6-1`](#rfc4271-5.1.6-1)

A BGP speaker that receives a route with the ATOMIC_AGGREGATE attribute MUST NOT make any NLRI of that route more specific (as defined in 9.1.4) when advertising this route to other BGP speakers. (§5.1.6)

Records cover only part of this requirement: tested "make any NLRI of that route more specific"; gap "advertising this route to other BGP speakers"; legacy IPv4 relay controls preserve exact NLRI during attribute rebuilds and retain independently received more-specific routes. A recorded admission bypass proves the negative raw-export /24-to-/25 check on ordinary and native route-server sockets with the answer marker retained or stripped: all six cases pass clean, then all four marked cases emit forbidden /25 output while both unmarked controls pass. These records do not prove MP NLRI, all native families, stored-route replay or prior-advertisement withdrawal. The restricted boundary is internal/component/bgp/reactor/filter_atomic_aggregate.go::atomicAggregatePolicyAllowed; the stronger product restriction is documented in docs/architecture/bgp/egress-attribute-rules.md; they do not verify the whole requirement.

Audit verdict: partial (the tests enforce only the declared tested scope; the whole requirement remains unmet), fresh. Independent scoped rejudge after rereading RFC 4271 Sections 5.1.6 and 9.1.4, the current partial annotation, all three tagged units, their socket relay helpers, runLibraryChain and both producing boundaries. Section 5.1.6 binds the route as received with ATOMIC_AGGREGATE even when export policy strips the answer marker; Section 9.1.4 forbids deaggregation. Tested is "make any NLRI of that route more specific"; Gap is "advertising this route to other BGP speakers"; Producer is internal/component/bgp/reactor/filter_atomic_aggregate.go::atomicAggregatePolicyAllowed. The release restriction accepts only original native identities and a retained marker for a marked input that still announces; its equality rule is deliberately stronger than the RFC containment obligation. The legacy positive unit requires exact received /17 and /24 NLRI, zero-length ATOMIC_AGGREGATE and changed MED after an attribute-policy rebuild on ordinary and native route-server socket rails. The independent-specific control requires exact separately received unmarked /25 and /32 output on both rails, excluding a blanket prefix-length clamp. Their existing builder mutant covers remain distinct and do not by themselves prove an independently observed mutant failure on each relay rail. The added negative unit sends a valid received /24 through raw export-policy /25 replacement, with the output marker retained and stripped on each rail; its two unmarked controls require exact /25 output and no withdrawals, while its four marked cases forbid covered more-specific socket output and permit safe suppression or preservation. Direct read of job-atomic-rfc-rewrite-record-f3037485.log confirms all six clean cases pass, then all four marked cases fail on forbidden decoded /25 socket output under the recorded admission bypass while both unmarked controls still pass. Canonical discrimination retains these three distinct covers; this rejudge creates no new mutant observation. These tags and records establish only the declared legacy IPv4 scope. They do not prove MP NLRI, every native family, stored-route replay, prior-advertisement withdrawal or broad ownership/recovery ordering. Those advertising paths remain unmet or unproven for this whole requirement. Verdict stays partial and earns zero whole-requirement credit.

### [`RFC4271-6.1-1`](#rfc4271-6.1-1)

All errors detected while processing the Message Header MUST be indicated by sending the NOTIFICATION message with the Error Code Message Header Error. (§6.1)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Every Section 6.1 error class is now driven through a running Peer's real read path in OpenSent and each answer is asserted as exactly one NOTIFICATION with Error Code 1 before EOF: marker all zeros / one 0xFE octet (1/1), Length 18 and 0 (1/2), Length 4097 and the OPEN 28 / UPDATE 22 / KEEPALIVE 20 / NOTIFICATION 20 per-type minima (1/2), Type 99 (1/3). Negatives: a well-formed OPEN in OpenSent, and a KEEPALIVE of 19 plus an UPDATE of 23 in Established, draw no NOTIFICATION and keep the connection. Records observed red: notifyHeaderErr, ValidateLengthWithMax, handleUnknownType (positives); handleOpen, handleUpdate (negatives). Re-read 2026-10-01 (c10 judge): the only change under this verdict is a tag comment line for another id (RFC4271-6.7-1 / RFC4271-Security-1 added, RFC4271-9.1.2.1-1 removed) in the unit's doc comment; every assertion is byte-identical, so the judgement stands.

### [`RFC4271-6.1-3`](#rfc4271-6.1-3)

If at least one of the following is true: - if the Length field of the message header is less than 19 or greater than 4096, or - if the Length field of an OPEN message is less than the minimum length of the OPEN message, or - if the Length field of an UPDATE message is less than the minimum length of the UPDATE message, or - if the Length field of a KEEPALIVE message is not equal to 19, or - if the Length field of a NOTIFICATION message is less than the minimum length of the NOTIFICATION message, then the Error Subcode MUST be set to Bad Message Length. The Data field MUST contain the erroneous Length field. (§6.1)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Re-judged 2026-10-01 (BGP c11 judge) after the negative's NOTIFICATION subtest moved from OpenSent to OpenConfirm (OpenSent now answers Event 25 with 5/0 per Section 8.2.2, so 'no NOTIFICATION at all' is only true in OpenConfirm); the 21-octet bound under test is unchanged. Positives prove every Section 6.1 Length condition with exactly one NOTIFICATION 1/2 whose Data is the received Length octets, then EOF: Length 18 and 0, 4097, OPEN 28, UPDATE 22, KEEPALIVE 20, NOTIFICATION 20. Negatives: KEEPALIVE 19 and UPDATE 23 and UPDATE 4096 in Established draw no NOTIFICATION; OPEN 29 in OpenSent draws none; a 21-octet Cease in OpenConfirm draws none and closes. Records observed red: ValidateLengthWithMax (re-recorded for the moved subtest), notifyHeaderErr, handleUpdate, handleOpen.

### [`RFC4271-6.2-3`](#rfc4271-6.2-3)

All errors detected while processing the OPEN message MUST be indicated by sending the NOTIFICATION message with the Error Code OPEN Message Error. (§6.2)

Records cover only part of this requirement: tested "NOTIFICATION message with the Error Code OPEN Message Error"; gap "All errors detected"; standard and RFC 9072 outer OPEN framing failures now produce exact NOTIFICATION 2/0 through the ordinary decoder and pending-connection reader; exact-length OPEN controls advance the ordinary handshake, while a valid pending collision retains Cease 6/7. These matrices do not bound every detected OPEN validation class; internal/component/bgp/reactor/session_handlers.go::handleOpen remains the gap-context for the full all-errors obligation. A nine-octet OPEN body is a message-header length error, not the Section 6.2 framing witness; they do not verify the whole requirement.

Audit verdict: partial (the tests enforce only the declared tested scope; the whole requirement remains unmet), fresh. RFC 4271 Section 6.2 says: All errors detected while processing the OPEN message MUST be indicated by sending the NOTIFICATION message with the Error Code OPEN Message Error. Tested is "NOTIFICATION message with the Error Code OPEN Message Error"; Gap is "All errors detected"; Producer is internal/component/bgp/reactor/session_handlers.go::handleOpen. The ordinary and pending matrices present three standard/RFC9072 outer-length failures with legal message-header lengths. Both now reach Session.admitOpen, UnpackOpen and rejectOpenCapabilityError; they require exact NOTIFICATION 2/0, ordinary Idle or retained incumbent OpenConfirm, and cleared pending admission. The pending fixture first reads its independent connection's local OPEN before sending malformed input, preserving the refusal assertions. Exact-length ordinary controls require KEEPALIVE and OpenConfirm; the valid pending collision requires Cease 6/7 and an unchanged incumbent. These controls distinguish framing refusal from unconditional OPEN or collision refusal. The handleOpen extraction delegates decoding and validation without changing this tested obligation. The matrices do not enumerate every OPEN validation class, assert TCP EOF independently, or prove universal incoming admission; this remains partial with zero whole-requirement credit.

### [`RFC4271-6.7-1`](#rfc4271-6.7-1)

However, the Cease NOTIFICATION message MUST NOT be used when a fatal error indicated by this section does exist. (§6.7)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Independently re-judged against RFC 4271 Sections 6, 6.6 and 6.7, RFC 6608 Section 4, all ten tagged units and the completed native renewal log job-rfc6608-record-renewals-09a8e39e.log. The prohibition concerns the fatal-error code, not an obligation to choose Cease for every discretionary closure. The running-peer tests distinguish all five Section 6 fatal classes: header code 1, OPEN code 2, UPDATE code 3, Hold Timer Expired code 4 and FSM code 5; each relevant exact-response assertion excludes Cease. The updated FSM expectations are 5/1 in OpenSent and 5/2 in OpenConfirm, as RFC 6608 requires; original error inputs, session release, timer and counter assertions remain. The BFD strict second-OPEN test independently asserts wire Error Code 5 without claiming its whole subcode or teardown outcome. The well-formed prefix-limit case provides the distinct Cease 6/1 control permitted by Section 6.7. TestRFC4271UpdateErrorReportedAsUpdateMessageError and TestPrefixExceedTeardown remain supplementary tagged checks; the refreshed records do not manufacture records for those older units. Whole-producer reverts prove dependence on their named producer, while the exact code assertions supply the semantic distinction. No one-octet RFC 6608 Data claim is credited here.

### [`RFC4271-8.2.1-3`](#rfc4271-8.2.1-3)

For each incoming connection, a state machine MUST be instantiated (§8.2.1)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Independently rejudged against RFC 4271 Sections 8, 8.2.1, 8.2.1.2 and 6.7, the relevant RFC 4486 Section 4 refusal distinction, RFC 6286 collision updates, RFC 6608 unexpected-message handling, RFC 7606 UPDATE handling and the RFC Editor inline-errata rendition. Section 8 states: "The data structures and FSM described in this document are conceptual and do not have to be implemented precisely as described here, as long as the implementations support the described functionality and they exhibit the same externally visible behavior." Section 8.2.1's incoming requirement therefore binds connection behavior, not the presence of a particular Session/FSM object. The positive listener unit receives Ze's OPEN on the admitted incoming TCP connection before sending its remote OPEN and completes that connection's OPEN/KEEPALIVE exchange while the outgoing connection has received no remote OPEN. The separate negative reaches EOF on a held incoming connection, observes exactly one retry increment and released pending ownership, then establishes the original held connection on its original TCP socket. These are useful success and failure-isolation outcomes, not object-count assertions alone. Both current native records replace newIncomingSession's isolated exchange with the incumbent: main observed each clean named unit PASS and each broken named unit FAIL, respectively missing incoming OPEN and missing the candidate's held DelayOpen state. The input isolates this per-connection ownership obligation and the two polarities are separate functions. Reachable producers preserve the incoming Session, reader and timer lifecycle through collision selection; local capacity refusal before that exchange is permitted by Section 6.7 and RFC 4486 Section 4 and does not owe an otherwise unused FSM object. Current capacity refusal sends 6/5; an actual OPEN-based collision loser retains 6/7. Additional untagged capacity controls observed by main exercise refusal/EOF, the same admitted connection's true collision outcome and useful incumbent traffic. They support the refusal interpretation without becoming extra tagged whole-row proof. This judgment covers the stated incoming handshake and failure ownership obligation; it does not claim every plugin callback, every BGP requirement or live interoperability has been proven. A separately identified provisional role-callback publication defect remains owned by its correction and independent closure work. Earlier behavioral reclassification rationale retained: The earlier partial finding inferred a universal conformance gap solely because refused additional TCP sockets did not construct Session/FSM objects. That inference contradicted RFC 4271 Section 8's explicit conceptual-structure provision and ignored Section 6.7 and RFC 4486 Section 4's permitted local refusal behavior. Re-read the primary text, actual admission/handshake/retirement producers, both complete tagged bodies and renewed native records. The existing bodies already discriminate the required per-connection handshake and isolated failure behavior; correcting their claim wording and withdrawing the structural-only gap warrants enforced rather than constructing unused objects or requiring unlimited incoming admission. All existing assertions remain intact. Final closure rejudgment: independently reread RFC 4271 Sections 8, 8.2.1, 8.2.1.2, 6.7 and 9, all three current tagged functions and their assertion-producing helpers, and the complete current incoming factory/read/adopt paths. The existing positive incoming handshake and negative EOF/failure-isolation outcomes remain distinct behavioral proofs. The added positive TestIncomingBeforeOpenUpdateCannotPublishIncumbentWithdrawal uses two actual listener sockets and the real delivery worker in both receive modes. With the new connection held in Active before OPEN, its otherwise valid withdrawal must reach neither received-UPDATE callback nor delivery queue; an Established sibling retains its modeled route through a legal FIFO fence, then delivers its own legitimate withdrawal exactly once. The body expressly models route presence from worker delivery and makes no actual Adj-RIB-In or Loc-RIB claim. The shared updateIsUnexpected producer now admits UPDATEs only in Established, matching Section 9, before either rail publishes semantics; add that helper to the code fingerprint map. The private decoded OPEN pair likewise remains connection-owned until validated selection, while raw wire capture and physical OPEN counters retain their original boundaries. These source corrections strengthen externally visible connection isolation without reinstating a structural object-count obligation. Earlier historical wording about a separately owned publication defect describes the prior checkpoint; the current producer isolation is the subject of this final re-read. Native discrimination receipts are recorded separately; this source judgment does not claim tests or interop were executed by the auditor.
