If the HoldTimer_Expires event occurs (Event 10), the local system: - sends a NOTIFICATION message with the Error Code Hold Timer Expired, - sets the ConnectRetryTimer to zero (§8.2.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. RFC 4271 Section 8.2.2 Established Event 10 requires a Hold Timer Expired NOTIFICATION and ConnectRetryTimer zero. Re-read all four tagged units: HoldExpiryRunsTheEvent10ActionList and NoHoldExpiryLeavesTheSessionIntact drive real Session.Run, with event10Established explicitly arming ConnectRetryTimer before the expiry/no-expiry split; the positive now requires the exact ErrHoldTimerExpired sentinel, a stopped ConnectRetryTimer, and code 4 on the captured closing connection, while the negative retains the armed timer and Established session. EstablishedHoldTimerExpiryRunsTheEvent10List additionally drives a running Peer and requireReleased demands exactly one 4/0, EOF and stopped timers; EstablishedHoldTimerFedByKeepalivesNeverExpires supplies KEEPALIVEs for three hold times and demands no NOTIFICATION, no close and the same live session. The peer timer alone starts unarmed, but the explicitly armed Session pair makes deletion of timer stopping observable. Producers are the NewSession hold-expiry callback, Run/retireSessionTimers, and fsm.Timers.StopAll/Retire; the sentinel assertion strengthens causal isolation rather than replacing any action assertion. Source judgment only; no native execution in this audit.

### [`RFC4271-8.2.2-3`](#rfc4271-8.2.2-3)

If the HoldTimer_Expires event occurs (Event 10), the local system: - sends a NOTIFICATION message with the Error Code Hold Timer Expired, - sets the ConnectRetryTimer to zero, - releases all BGP resources (§8.2.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Read the complete Established Event 10 list in RFC 4271 Section 8.2.2 and all four tagged units. HoldExpiryRunsTheEvent10ActionList requires exact ErrHoldTimerExpired, the HoldTimer and KeepaliveTimer stopped, ConnectRetryTimer stopped and the closing connection's code-4 NOTIFICATION; its no-expiry counterpart retains all armed timers and Established state. EstablishedHoldTimerExpiryRunsTheEvent10List and its KEEPALIVE-fed counterpart exercise the running Peer: requireReleased checks exactly one 4/0, release of the session object and all three session timers, with the healthy control retaining the same session, timers and connection over three hold times. These cover release of the per-connection resources held by these sessions, not merely successful Run completion. The RFC separately names route deletion in neighboring Event 18/24/25 lists; no new claim about a populated RIB is inferred from these empty-route fixtures. Run retires timers and closeConn retires transport, while the FSM transition releases the Peer session. The exact-sentinel edit removes no resource assertion. Source judgment only; no native red or green claimed.

### [`RFC4271-8.2.2-4`](#rfc4271-8.2.2-4)

If the HoldTimer_Expires event occurs (Event 10), the local system: - sends a NOTIFICATION message with the Error Code Hold Timer Expired, - sets the ConnectRetryTimer to zero, - releases all BGP resources, - drops the TCP connection (§8.2.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Re-judged 2026-11-14 by an independent auditor who authored none of 178cffd97d, 1c6795e9e4, 43cfa9af6e, after session_wedge_test.go::TestHoldExpiryClosesBehindStuckWriter joined the row as a third positive. RFC 4271 Section 8.2.2 (Established) read at rfc/full/rfc4271.txt:3988-4006: "If the HoldTimer_Expires event occurs (Event 10), the local system: - sends a NOTIFICATION message with the Error Code Hold Timer Expired, - sets the ConnectRetryTimer to zero, - releases all BGP resources, - drops the TCP connection,". The new unit drives a real Session to Established over net.Pipe with Run executing (wedgeEstablished), stops the keepalive timer so the hold expiry is the only callback the fake-clock advance runs, takes writeMu as a writer blocked in a socket write holds it, fires the production hold expiry, and requires the peer end to read io.EOF (errors.Is, a timeout is t.Fatal) within holdExpiryNotifyDeadline (1s) plus 2s slack; after the release Run must return ErrHoldTimerExpired and the FSM be Idle. Q1: it asserts the RFC clause "drops the TCP connection" as the peer observes it, not an internal flag. Q2: a close that waits for the write path leaves the pipe open and the select times out; rfc/discrimination/rfc4271/RFC4271-8.2.2-4.json records the red under a disabled dropConn (session_connection.go). Q3: EOF can only come from ze closing its end (the fixture's own close yields io.ErrClosedPipe, which the assertion refuses), and ErrHoldTimerExpired pins the hold expiry as the cause. Q4: the unit proves the drop clause alone, in the one state where the NOTIFICATION cannot be written; its claim says exactly that (EOF within the bound, write path held) and claims neither the NOTIFICATION, the timer nor the release. Those clauses stay with the four earlier units, whose fingerprints did not move: HoldExpiryRunsTheEvent10ActionList and EstablishedHoldTimerExpiryRunsTheEvent10List pin code 4 read from the connection before closure, the timer stops, session release and EOF; NoHoldExpiryLeavesTheSessionIntact and EstablishedHoldTimerFedByKeepalivesNeverExpires are the negatives. Whole requirement enforced across the five. Observation, not a finding against the row: with the write path held no NOTIFICATION reaches the wire, which RFC 4271 cannot require of a socket that accepts no write; the connection drop is what remains owed and is what the unit proves. Native commands were not run by the auditor.

### [`RFC4271-8.2.2-5`](#rfc4271-8.2.2-5)

If the HoldTimer_Expires event occurs (Event 10), the local system: - sends a NOTIFICATION message with the Error Code Hold Timer Expired, - sets the ConnectRetryTimer to zero, - releases all BGP resources, - drops the TCP connection, - increments the ConnectRetryCounter by 1, - (optionally) performs peer oscillation damping if the DampPeerOscillations attribute is set to TRUE, and - changes its state to Idle. (§8.2.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. RFC 4271 Section 8.2.2 Established Event 10 requires the complete NOTIFICATION/zero/release/drop/increment/Idle action list; peer-oscillation damping is optional. All four tagged units were re-read. The Session expiry unit now requires exact ErrHoldTimerExpired as well as stopped Hold/Keepalive/ConnectRetry timers, Idle, a closed connection and code-4 NOTIFICATION; its no-expiry unit retains the timers, connection and Established state. The running-Peer expiry unit's requireReleased observes exactly one 4/0 then EOF, release of the Peer session and all three timers, and ConnectRetryCounter exactly 0 to 1, followed by exact Idle. The KEEPALIVE-fed Peer control runs three hold times with no NOTIFICATION or close, the same session and Established state, and counter exactly zero. fsm's Established EventHoldTimerExpires branch increments once and changes to Idle; Run and connection teardown retire the resources. The older Session unit alone does not assert the counter, but the separately tagged Peer pair does. Damping is not claimed. The sentinel edit strengthens the expiry cause without dropping any clause. No native execution claimed.

### [`RFC4271-8.2.2-7`](#rfc4271-8.2.2-7)

In response to a ManualStart event (Event 1) or an AutomaticStart event (Event 3), the local system: - initializes all BGP resources for the peer connection, - sets ConnectRetryCounter to zero (§8.2.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. After the R45 split the Event 3 clause is RFC4271-8.2.2-20 ({gap}); this verdict judges the ManualStart (Event 1) clause the row keeps. Positive at peer level: Peer.Start on a dial-only Peer at counter 7 builds a session, dials (the far end reads an OPEN: resources initialized) and reads counter 0; StartWithContext break observed red. Negative: TestRFC4271ConnectRetryCounterSurvivesDampedStart, Event 6 from Idle and Events 1/6 in Connect and Active leave counter 7, so the reset is not spread to other start paths; handleIdle break observed red (recorded by this judge). The row text still reads 'or an AutomaticStart event (Event 3)', which no tag claims.

### [`RFC4271-8.2.2-20`](#rfc4271-8.2.2-20)

or an AutomaticStart event (Event 3), the local system: - initializes all BGP resources for the peer connection, - sets ConnectRetryCounter to zero (§8.2.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Independent rejudgment after rereading RFC 4271 Sections 8.1.2, 8.2.1.5 and 8.2.2 Idle, all eight current tagged units and their assertion-producing helpers, and the reached FSM, Session and Peer start producers. Idle Event 3 resets counter 7 to exactly zero in active and passive modes; Event 6 and Event 3 outside Idle retain counter/state. Real Peer positive cycles start a new useful connection after Established exits by received Cease or actual HoldTimer expiry and observe counter 1 before restart and exactly zero after the new OPEN. Failed OpenSent attempts preserve exactly one across a next dial, including an unsuccessful cycle following an earlier Established cycle; these distinguish resetting every cycle from consuming the prior Established result once. Real inbound-notify controls hold actual backoff and prove the new socket reaches OpenSent with zero after actual HoldTimer expiry, versus one after a never-Established failure. The passive positive observes exact leave-Established count one and a distinct Active Session with zero after Cease, real expiry or automatic teardown; its teardown helper verifies Cease and ErrTeardown. Peer.runOnce consumes cycleReachedEstablished with Swap(false), defaults to the damped start, selects the automatic start after Established, and overrides only the first operator cycle. Its factory attaches the peer-owned counter before any start. Removing the unused emitFSMEvents event argument does not change this selection or any test assertion. RFC 4271 Section 8.2.1.5 says, "The details of these actions are outside the scope of the FSM document." Initialization is observed through creation and use of each next connection/Session, not an invented exhaustive resource inventory. The current short and derived row contain no gap or partial annotation and the derived row lists all eight covers. Runtime discrimination freshness is checked separately before stamping this reread; this note claims no new execution.

### [`RFC4271-8.2.2-9`](#rfc4271-8.2.2-9)

If the HoldTimer_Expires event occurs (Event 10), the local system: - sends a NOTIFICATION message with the Error Code Hold Timer Expired, - sets the ConnectRetryTimer to zero, - releases all BGP resources, - drops the TCP connection, - increments the ConnectRetryCounter by 1 (§8.2.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Independently rejudged after the enum-owned claim changed 'default arm' to 'named ignore arm'. Read the full Section 8.2.2 in rfc/full/rfc4271.txt, including the whole Established sentence: 'If the HoldTimer_Expires event occurs (Event 10), the local system: - sends a NOTIFICATION message with the Error Code Hold Timer Expired, - sets the ConnectRetryTimer to zero, - releases all BGP resources, - drops the TCP connection, - increments the ConnectRetryCounter by 1, - (optionally) performs peer oscillation damping if the DampPeerOscillations attribute is set to TRUE, and - changes its state to Idle.' The OpenSent and OpenConfirm paragraphs prescribe the corresponding action list. All four tagged units were read: fsm/rfc4271_connect_retry_test.go::TestRFC4271ConnectRetryCounterIncrementsOnHoldTimerExpiry checks exactly 3 to 4 and Idle in OpenSent, OpenConfirm and Established; TestRFC4271ConnectRetryCounterQuietOnHealthyEstablishedTraffic checks unchanged counter 3 for the enumerated healthy events and Idle hold expiry. reactor/rfc4271_fsm_teardown_peer_test.go::TestRFC4271OpenSentHoldTimerExpiryReleasesTheConnection arms the session HoldTimer to 150 ms, sends no OPEN and, through requireReleased, asserts exactly one NOTIFICATION 4/0, connection closure, session release, stopped timers and counter 0 to 1. reactor/rfc4271_opensent_error_peer_test.go::TestRFC4271OpenSentWellFormedOpenKeepsTheConnection supplies a valid OPEN before expiry and asserts no NOTIFICATION, live connection, same session and counter 3. Read timer.go::fireHold, session.go::NewSession's shared hold callback and Run cleanup, session_read.go::handleConnectionClose, peer_run.go::runOnce's release defer, and all three FSM hold-expiry arms. The common callback sends 4/0, fires EventHoldTimerExpires and signals errChan; Run consumes that signal and releases the connection and timers. handleIdle's explicit ignore branch preserves the previous result and matches Section 8.2.2: 'Any other event (Events 9-12, 15-28) received in the Idle state does not cause change in the state of the local system.' The saved diff changes no assertion. Evidence boundary: wire-level assertions here are OpenSent, with FSM counter/state coverage in all three states, not three independent wire-level runs. ConnectRetryTimer is already unarmed in this production lifecycle; its stopped-state assertion does not independently demonstrate cancellation of an armed timer. Static call-site search found StartConnectRetryTimer only in tests and its declaration. Stored fireHold/handleOpenSent discrimination records were read, not renewed or verified by this audit; no new green or red execution is claimed.

### [`RFC4271-8.2.2-10`](#rfc4271-8.2.2-10)

If the BGP message header checking (Event 21) or OPEN message checking detects an error (Event 22)(see Section 6.2), the local system: - sends a NOTIFICATION message with the appropriate error code, - sets the ConnectRetryTimer to zero, - releases all BGP resources, - drops the TCP connection, - increments the ConnectRetryCounter by 1 (§8.2.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Independently rejudged after two enum-owned claims changed 'default arm' to 'error arm' and 'named ignore arm'. RFC 4271 Section 8.2.2, OpenSent, states the whole action sentence: 'If the BGP message header checking (Event 21) or OPEN message checking detects an error (Event 22)(see Section 6.2), the local system: - sends a NOTIFICATION message with the appropriate error code, - sets the ConnectRetryTimer to zero, - releases all BGP resources, - drops the TCP connection, - increments the ConnectRetryCounter by 1, - (optionally) performs peer oscillation damping if the DampPeerOscillations attribute is TRUE, and - changes its state to Idle.' All four tagged units were read. fsm/rfc4271_connect_retry_test.go::TestRFC4271ConnectRetryCounterIncrementsOnHeaderAndOpenErrors checks counter 0 to 1 and Idle for Events 21 and 22 in every non-Idle state; TestRFC4271ConnectRetryCounterNotIncrementedByIdleErrors checks unchanged counter 2, Idle and no error for the listed ignored events. reactor/rfc4271_opensent_error_peer_test.go::TestRFC4271OpenSentErrorReleasesTheConnection isolates an all-zero marker, Length 18, and OPEN version 3, respectively expecting exactly one 1/1, 1/2 or 2/1 NOTIFICATION, connection closure, session release, stopped timers and counter 0 to 1. TestRFC4271OpenSentWellFormedOpenKeepsTheConnection supplies a version-4 AS4 OPEN and asserts no NOTIFICATION for 500 ms, live connection, same session and unchanged counter 3. Read session_read.go::readAndProcessMessage and notifyHeaderErr, session_handlers.go::handleOpen, FSM.Event and all five consuming handlers, session.go::Run and peer_run.go::runOnce cleanup. Event 22 still reaches an incrementing ErrFSMError arm in Established; this supplementary counter assertion is not a claim that Established sends OpenSent's notification code. The isolated invalid inputs and conforming OPEN distinguish acceptance from refusal, and no assertion was changed in the saved diff. Evidence is for the named header/version errors, not an exhaustive enumeration of every OPEN validation failure. ConnectRetryTimer is unarmed in the production lifecycle, so stopped-state assertions preserve that invariant rather than prove armed-timer cancellation. Stored notifyHeaderErr/handleOpenSent discrimination records were inspected only; the changed claim paragraphs still owe the parent's native evidence renewal, including where no old FSM-unit record exists. No new test result or current fingerprint verification is claimed.

### [`RFC4271-8.2.2-11`](#rfc4271-8.2.2-11)

If the local system receives a TcpConnectionFails event (Event 18) from the underlying TCP or a NOTIFICATION message (Event 25), the local system: - sets the ConnectRetryTimer to zero, - releases all BGP resources, - drops the TCP connection, - increments the ConnectRetryCounter by 1 (§8.2.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Independently rejudged after the enum-owned claim changed 'default (FSM Error) arm' to 'named FSM Error arm'. RFC 4271 Section 8.2.2, OpenConfirm, states the whole sentence: 'If the local system receives a TcpConnectionFails event (Event 18) from the underlying TCP or a NOTIFICATION message (Event 25), the local system: - sets the ConnectRetryTimer to zero, - releases all BGP resources, - drops the TCP connection, - increments the ConnectRetryCounter by 1, - (optionally) performs peer oscillation damping if the DampPeerOscillations attribute is set to TRUE, and - changes its state to Idle.' Read all four tagged units. fsm/rfc4271_connect_retry_test.go::TestRFC4271ConnectRetryCounterIncrementsOnNotification checks Event 25 gives counter 1 and Idle in all non-Idle states, with ErrFSMError specifically in OpenSent and no error elsewhere. TestRFC4271ConnectRetryCounterStepsByExactlyOnePerNotification checks cumulative counts 1 through 10 using the same counter across Established FSM instances; this is supplementary exact-step evidence, not the non-triggering input of the semantic pair. reactor/rfc4271_fsm_teardown_peer_test.go::TestRFC4271OpenConfirmNotificationOrTCPFailureReleasesTheConnection runs separate received-Cease and far-end TCP-close cases, asserting session release, stopped timers and counter 0 to 1; the NOTIFICATION case also reads connection closure and no reply NOTIFICATION. TestRFC4271OpenConfirmKeepaliveKeepsTheConnection supplies the genuinely non-triggering KEEPALIVE, asserts Established, no NOTIFICATION for 500 ms, live connection, same session, running HoldTimer and counter 0. Read session_handlers.go::handleNotification and fsmMessageEvent, session_read.go::handleConnectionClose, session.go::Run, peer_run.go::runOnce cleanup, connect_retry_counter.go::Increment, and the FSM dispatch/handlers. Events 18 and 25 reach separate explicit OpenConfirm arms that each increment once and enter Idle; message handling and Run stop timers and close the connection. The new named OpenSent Event 25 arm preserves the prior ErrFSMError result and does not silently drop the event. Assertions are unchanged in the saved diff. Evidence boundary: the TCP-close case closes the far-end socket itself and does not read that socket afterward, so it is not an independent wire observation of 'no reply'; the row itself requires no reply notification. ConnectRetryTimer starts unarmed in production. Stored handleOpenSent/handleOpenConfirm records were inspected, not renewed; no new green, red or freshness result is claimed.

### [`RFC4271-8.2.2-12`](#rfc4271-8.2.2-12)

If the local system receives a NOTIFICATION message (Event 24 or Event 25) or a TcpConnectionFails (Event 18) from the underlying TCP, the local system: - sets the ConnectRetryTimer to zero, - deletes all routes associated with this connection, - releases all the BGP resources, - drops the TCP connection, - increments the ConnectRetryCounter by 1 (§8.2.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), stale-unit: internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rfc4760_peer_down_test.go::TestRFC4760PeerDownDeletesOnlyThatNeighborsRoutes moved. Re-judged 2026-10-31 (independent auditor). Row: RFC 4271 Section 8.2.2 Established, Event 24/25/18 list. c987777bb8 changed TestRFC4271ConnectRetryCounterOnVersionErrorPerState in its doc comment only (the Event 24 clause in Connect/Active now said to sit in the DelayOpenTimer-is-not-running branch the timer-disabled crcFSM fixture exercises); the body is unchanged and still requires counter exactly 1 and Idle after NotifMsgVerErr in Connect, Active and Established, while the negative requires OpenSent and OpenConfirm to leave the counter at 0. Peer-level units unchanged: NOTIFICATION 2/1 (Event 24), Cease (Event 25) and far-end close (Event 18) in Established each raise peer-down, drop the session, stop the timers and move the counter 0 -> 1; KEEPALIVE plus a valid UPDATE keep the session (negative). Routes clause: TestRFC4760PeerDownDeletesOnlyThatNeighborsRoutes deletes that neighbor's routes and keeps a bystander's; the dispatcher translation from reactor peer-down to the RIB event is not asserted end to end. Correction to the previous note: fsm Timers.StartConnectRetryTimer now has production callers (b6d84e8104: Connect Event 18 with a running DelayOpenTimer, Active Event 18), and FSM.change stops the ConnectRetryTimer on every Idle entry except Active Event 18; the peer-level 'ConnectRetryTimer not running' assertion therefore guards a reachable timer, not only a future one. Tagged units ran green 2026-10-31 under -race. Verdict unchanged: enforced.

### [`RFC4271-8.2.2-13`](#rfc4271-8.2.2-13)

If the local system receives a TcpConnectionFails event (Event 18), the local system: - restarts the ConnectRetryTimer (with the initial value), - stops and clears the DelayOpenTimer (sets the value to zero), - releases all BGP resource, - increments the ConnectRetryCounter by 1 (§8.2.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Independent rejudgment of the Active Event 18 requirement after reading RFC 4271 Section 8.2.2 Idle, Connect and Active, every tagged unit, and the reached Session/FSM/Peer retry producers. The held-Active positive and the separate ActiveDelayOpenBfdUpPending positive both use a real selected passive Peer with a net.Pipe initial transport, seed a nonzero counter, close that transport to deliver EOF through the production reader, observe old Idle/session/connection retirement and exactly one increment, record the actual Peer wait within the jittered initial ConnectRetryTime interval, release that wait and establish a distinct useful replacement over real TCP without resetting history. The strict positive first proves strict Event 20 cleared DelayOpen while retaining Active, and then checks DelayOpen, ConnectRetry, Hold, Keepalive and BFD hold timers stopped; it closes the formerly uncovered unconditional Active branch. The OpenSent negative sends an actual OPEN before EOF, observes Active with unchanged history and the configured ordinary interval, then establishes a useful replacement. Session.handleConnectionCloseLocked transfers the actual uncanceled FSM retry after any actual Event 18, before StopAll; Peer.runOnce joins that owner before reading the transferred budget, and Peer.run waits on its original-clock remaining deadline. Thus the tests distinguish required initial retry from ordinary backoff, timer cleanup from premature timer cancellation, and increment from fixed assignment. These units do not measure kernel TCP close behavior. This verdict concerns the quoted row through its counter clause; the following optional damping clause is outside this row. Final reread confirms all three native clause records, the current tagged units and producers, and removal of the obsolete gap annotation in both the short and derived rows.

### [`RFC4271-8.2.2-14`](#rfc4271-8.2.2-14)

If the local system receives an UPDATE message, and the UPDATE message error handling procedure (see Section 6.3) detects an error (Event 28), the local system: - sends a NOTIFICATION message with an Update error, - sets the ConnectRetryTimer to zero, - deletes all routes associated with this connection, - releases all BGP resources, - drops the TCP connection, - increments the ConnectRetryCounter by 1 (§8.2.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), stale-unit: internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rfc4760_peer_down_test.go::TestRFC4760PeerDownDeletesOnlyThatNeighborsRoutes moved. Peer level in Established: an UPDATE with a malformed MP_REACH_NLRI (an error RFC 7606 still answers with a session reset, Event 28) draws exactly one NOTIFICATION with Error Code 3, EOF, the peer-down for that neighbor, the session dropped, three timers stopped, counter 0 -> 1; the RIB unit proves the routes deletion on that peer-down. Negative: a well-formed UPDATE is dispatched with no peer-down, same session Established, counter 0. Records observed red: handleEstablished (positive), handleStructuredState (rib positive), handleUpdate (negative). Subcode not asserted (the row asks for 'an Update error', the code). Not asserted end to end: the plugin dispatcher's peer-down translation into the RIB event (same caveat as RFC4760-7-1). The ConnectRetryTimer clause holds by construction: fsm Timers.StartConnectRetryTimer has no non-test caller, so the peer-level 'not running' assertion guards a future arming, and the HEAD rfc4271_test.go unit arms it and proves StopAll stops it. 2026-10-01 re-judge (BGP c9 judge, wrote none of these units): stale only because TestRFC4271EstablishedUpdateErrorReleasesTheConnection gained one RFC4271-6.3-1 tag comment line; no executable line moved. Verdict unchanged. Re-read 2026-10-01 (c10 judge): the only change under this verdict is a tag comment line for another id (RFC4271-6.7-1 / RFC4271-Security-1 added, RFC4271-9.1.2.1-1 removed) in the unit's doc comment; every assertion is byte-identical, so the judgement stands.

### [`RFC4271-8.2.2-15`](#rfc4271-8.2.2-15)

In response to any other events (Events 8, 10-11, 13, 19, 23, 25-28), the local system: - if the ConnectRetryTimer is running, stops and resets the ConnectRetryTimer (sets to zero), - if the DelayOpenTimer is running, stops and resets the DelayOpenTimer (sets to zero), - releases all BGP resources, - drops the TCP connection, - increments the ConnectRetryCounter by 1 (§8.2.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Independent rejudgment after reading RFC 4271 Section 8.2.2 Connect and Active and all ten tagged units. The Connect list's declared events are exercised independently at the FSM boundary with exact Idle and counter 3-to-4; ignored starts and Connect retry expiry preserve their correct states/counters. The older pure-FSM positive checks the explicit listed-event groups with exact zero-to-one increments and Idle; its negative checks the explicit Idle ignore group with a seeded counter of six. Their corrected tags match the unchanged bodies. Real Peer units prove Connect dial-time AutomaticStop retires the socket/session with EOF and no octet, while ignored ManualStart keeps the same useful dial; Active units prove retirement and rejection of a later socket versus an unsealed ignored-start session. The new held-Connect KEEPALIVE unit proves DelayOpen was running and was stopped at the first published Idle transition before final Session retirement can mask its absence, then proves actual TCP EOF without output, old socket/session release, one counter increment and stopped remaining timers. Its ignored ManualStart control preserves the running DelayOpen and exact Session/socket and then establishes that same connection with conforming OPEN/KEEPALIVE. The first-Idle observation is reached synchronously in Peer.runOnce's FSM-history clock timestamp. FSM.change owns the shared DelayOpen and retry stop for Connect-to-Idle transitions. A running ConnectRetryTimer in canonical Connect is not currently a reachable session condition: its only arm callers are Connect Event 18, which enters Active, and Active Event 18, which enters Idle; successful TCP handling stops retry. The timer condition must be rejudged if a Connect arm is added. The absent optional IdleHoldTimer event is not claimed as exercised. Final reread confirms all ten native clause records and their current tagged units and producers; the derived row includes all ten covers.

### [`RFC4271-8.2.2-21`](#rfc4271-8.2.2-21)

In response to any other event (Events 8, 10-11, 13, 19, 23, 25-28), the local system: - sets the ConnectRetryTimer to zero, - releases all BGP resources, - drops the TCP connection, - increments the ConnectRetryCounter by one (§8.2.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Active 'any other event' list (verbatim span, R47(b) row). Peer level, running passive Peer in Active at counter 3: teardownAutomatic (Event 8) -> Idle, counter 4, HoldTimer/KeepaliveTimer/ConnectRetryTimer not running, Peer drops the session, a later TCP connection is refused with ErrSessionTearingDown and never held, counter stays 4. Negative: Event 1 (not in the list) leaves Active, counter 3 for 300 ms, same unsealed session. handleActive break observed red both polarities. Clause notes: 'sets the ConnectRetryTimer to zero' holds by construction (Timers.StartConnectRetryTimer has no non-test caller), so the 'not running' assertion guards a future arming. 'drops the TCP connection': Ze's Active holds no TCP connection, so the proof is that a TCP connection offered afterwards is refused and never becomes the session's; the FSM-level unit for 8.2.2-15 (TestRFC4271ConnectActiveAnyOtherEventCountsAndDropsToIdle) also drives Events 10, 11, 19, 23, 25-28 in Active but is not tagged here, so only Event 8 is proven at peer level for this row. Negative uses a start event (pair holds: Event 1 is ignored, not this list).

### [`RFC4271-8.2.2-24`](#rfc4271-8.2.2-24)

In response to any other event (Events 9, 11-13, 20, 25-28), the local system: - sends the NOTIFICATION with the Error Code Finite State Machine Error, - sets the ConnectRetryTimer to zero, - releases all BGP resources, - drops the TCP connection, - increments the ConnectRetryCounter by 1 (§8.2.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Independently re-judged from the complete RFC 4271 Section 8.2.2 OpenSent action list and RFC 6608 Section 4. TestRFC4271OpenSentUnexpectedMessageIsAnFSMError retains four distinct real TCP inputs: Cease NOTIFICATION (Event 25), KEEPALIVE (26), empty UPDATE (27), and malformed withdrawn-length UPDATE (28). Each requires exactly one 5/1 response, TCP closure rather than a read timeout, stopped ConnectRetry/Hold/Keepalive timers, released session and ConnectRetryCounter 0 to 1. The older readUntil helper accepts a non-timeout read error as closure; this note does not call that a strict io.EOF assertion. The shared FSM catchall producer contains the same retry-counter/state transition for the listed timer/connection events; the peer matrix directly exercises received-message events. TestRFC4271ExpectedMessagesRaiseNoFSMError distinguishes a valid OPEN and subsequent legitimate KEEPALIVE/UPDATE with a live retained session and counter zero. The separate Event 24 control sends 2/1, requires no NOTIFICATION, closure/release and unchanged nonzero counter 3. These boundary controls distinguish state and version-error classification rather than merely another malformed packet. The renewed native records retain clean-green and named-unit-red observations, including the version-error claim renewal. The updated-by marker correctly adds the RFC 6608 subcode/Data obligation without removing the base teardown list. These base tests do not assert the one-octet Data; the updater is audited separately.

### [`RFC4271-8.2.2-25`](#rfc4271-8.2.2-25)

In response to any other event (Events 9, 12-13, 20, 27-28), the local system: - sends a NOTIFICATION with a code of Finite State Machine Error, - sets the ConnectRetryTimer to zero, - releases all BGP resources, - drops the TCP connection, - increments the ConnectRetryCounter by 1 (§8.2.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Independently re-judged from the full RFC 4271 Section 8.2.2 OpenConfirm list and RFC 6608 Section 4. TestRFC4271OpenConfirmUpdateIsAnFSMError sends an empty UPDATE (Event 27) and the retained withdrawn-length overrun (Event 28) after OPEN and before KEEPALIVE. Each requires exactly one 5/2, TCP closure, three stopped session timers, session release and counter 0 to 1. The close helper distinguishes closure from a read deadline but does not require the concrete io.EOF value. updateIsUnexpected intercepts these inputs before UPDATE semantic parsing, and fsmMessageEvent preserves the receiving state for the RFC 6608 response; the shared FSM catchall still performs the listed counter/state actions. TestRFC4271ExpectedMessagesRaiseNoFSMError gives a conforming OpenConfirm KEEPALIVE that reaches Established and the same empty UPDATE accepted there, retaining connection/session and counter zero. Native renewals for the positive and existing boundary controls are green before their named producer breaks and red under those breaks; reverts establish dependence, not every possible fault. The updater changes the diagnostic subcode to 2 while the original teardown obligations remain. No separate credit is claimed here for RFC 6608's one-octet message Data.

### [`RFC4271-8.2.2-26`](#rfc4271-8.2.2-26)

If a NOTIFICATION message is received with a version error (Event 24), the local system: - sets the ConnectRetryTimer to zero, - releases all BGP resources, - drops the TCP connection, and - changes its state to Idle. (§8.2.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Independently re-judged against RFC 4271 Section 8.2.2 OpenSent Event 24's complete four-action list and Section 6.2's Unsupported Version Number definition, with RFC 6608 Section 4 applied to the non-version control. TestRFC4271VersionErrorNotificationReleasesQuietly/OPENSENT starts ConnectRetryCounter at 3, receives NOTIFICATION 2/1 and requires the exact empty outgoing notification list, TCP closure, ConnectRetry/Hold/Keepalive timers stopped, session released and Idle, with counter still 3. The initial value detects both an increment and an erroneous reset; the Event 24 list has no counter increment. The close helper treats a non-timeout read failure as closure, without pinning strict io.EOF. TestRFC4271OtherOpenErrorNotificationIsNotAVersionError/OPENSENT changes only the triggering subcode to 2/2 and requires Event 25's exact 5/1 response and counter 3 to 4 with teardown. Thus an error-code-only version classifier fails. The original buffers and teardown assertions are retained; the approved expectation change is RFC 6608's state diagnostic, not relaxed behavior. notificationEvent selects Event 24 only for 2/1 and handleOpenSent keeps the separate no-increment arm. The renewed negative record observes a clean named-unit pass followed by red under its producer break.

### [`RFC4271-8.2.2-27`](#rfc4271-8.2.2-27)

If the local system receives a NOTIFICATION message with a version error (NotifMsgVerErr (Event 24)), the local system: - sets the ConnectRetryTimer to zero, - releases all BGP resources, - drops the TCP connection, and - changes its state to Idle. (§8.2.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Independently re-judged against the entire RFC 4271 Section 8.2.2 OpenConfirm Event 24 action list and the neighbouring Event 18/25 list. TestRFC4271VersionErrorNotificationReleasesQuietly/OPENCONFIRM receives NOTIFICATION 2/1 at counter 3 and requires no outgoing NOTIFICATION, TCP closure, three stopped session timers, released session, Idle and counter still 3. The close helper establishes closure rather than a read timeout, not specifically io.EOF. TestRFC4271OtherOpenErrorNotificationIsNotAVersionError/OPENCONFIRM receives 2/2 with otherwise the same setup, still sends no NOTIFICATION but must increment 3 to 4, pinning the exact distinction between the version-error and ordinary-notification lists. The retained nonzero starting counter detects both an increment on the positive and a reset/omitted increment on the negative. notificationEvent and handleOpenConfirm implement that distinct classification and no-increment arm. Approved wording/subcode changes elsewhere in the shared units do not remove these OpenConfirm assertions. Native records and the completed renewal log remain producer-dependence evidence; the separate exact counter assertions establish the semantic boundary.

### [`RFC4271-8.2.2-28`](#rfc4271-8.2.2-28)

In response to any other event (Events 9, 12-13, 20-22), the local system: - sends a NOTIFICATION message with the Error Code Finite State Machine Error, - deletes all routes associated with this connection, - sets the ConnectRetryTimer to zero, - releases all BGP resources, - drops the TCP connection, - increments the ConnectRetryCounter by 1 (§8.2.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Judged 2026-10-01 (BGP c12 judge) under R49(a) and the dated correction: for Event 21 the Section 6.1 Message Header Error code governs the NOTIFICATION, the Section 8.2.2 Established list governs the teardown. Events 9, 12, 13, 20 cannot occur in Ze's Established, and Event 22 does not either (handleOpen answers any OPEN in Established with a Cease before checking it), so the claim covers Event 21 only and says so. Positive TestRFC4271EstablishedHeaderErrorReleasesTheConnection, subtests Marker (all-zero Marker, isolated: Length 19, Type KEEPALIVE) and Length (all-ones Marker, Length 18): exactly one NOTIFICATION 1/1 resp. 1/2, EOF, peer-down raised to the Reactor observers (the route-deletion trigger), session released, three timers stopped, counter 0->1. Negative TestRFC4271EstablishedValidHeaderKeepsTheConnection: a well-formed KEEPALIVE (Length 19, the Section 4.1 floor) gives no peer-down, same session, Established, counter 0. Records: positive on session_read.go::notifyHeaderErr, negative on message/header.go::ParseHeader, both observed red. The routes clause is proven to the peer-down raise; the RIB's deletion on that peer-down is proven by TestRFC4760PeerDownDeletesOnlyThatNeighborsRoutes (tagged RFC4271-8.2.2-12), the same chain 8.2.2-12 is judged on. 'FSM Error' itself is not asserted, by the R49(a) reading.

### [`RFC4271-5-7`](#rfc4271-5-7)

The sender of an UPDATE message SHOULD order path attributes within the UPDATE message in ascending order of attribute type. (§5) Context: RFC 7606 Section 5.1, which updates RFC 4271, says the MP_REACH_NLRI or MP_UNREACH_NLRI attribute SHALL be encoded as the very first path attribute; the ascending order applies to the attributes after it.

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Re-judged 2026-10-20 (independent auditor, authored none of the change). RFC 4271 Section 5: "The sender of an UPDATE message SHOULD order path attributes within the UPDATE message in ascending order of attribute type." RFC 7606 (header: "Updates: 1997, 4271, ...") Section 5.1: "The MP_REACH_NLRI or MP_UNREACH_NLRI attribute (if present) SHALL be encoded as the very first path attribute in an UPDATE message." f29e582ed4 changes the row text only: the verbatim Section 5 quote is unchanged and a Context note after (§5) says RFC 7606 Section 5.1, which updates RFC 4271, puts MP_REACH_NLRI or MP_UNREACH_NLRI first and the ascending order applies after it. Checked against rfc/full/rfc7606.txt: RFC 7606 lists 4271 in Updates, and the note paraphrases the 5.1 bullet faithfully (it omits "(if present)", which does not change what it asserts, and adds no obligation). The note answers the finding of the previous judgement and now matches what the tagged units assert (requireAscendingAttributes skips a leading code 14 then requires strictly ascending codes; TestSplitMP_PreservesAscendingAttributeOrder and the reactor rails assert exact sequences after a leading 14/15). No code, test or claim changed; the claims already said "after any leading MP_REACH_NLRI", so no claim exceeds its assertion. Scope unchanged: originating builders and split preservation. Verdict stands.

### [`RFC4271-6.3-2`](#rfc4271-6.3-2)

If the NEXT_HOP attribute is semantically incorrect, the error SHOULD be logged, and the route SHOULD be ignored. (§6.3)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), stale-unit: internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rfc4271_self_nexthop_test.go::TestRFC4271SelfNextHopRouteIsNotInstalled moved. Re-judged 2026-10-01 (BGP c15 judge). Both clauses proven at their producers. 'the route SHOULD be ignored': TestRFC4271SemanticallyIncorrectNextHopIsLoggedAndIgnored and TestRFC4271SemanticallyIncorrectNextHopIsLoggedAtTheDefaultLevel drive a live session read over a one-hop EBGP session; NEXT_HOP = the receiving speaker (criterion a) and NEXT_HOP off the shared subnet (criterion b) leave NLRI empty and move 203.0.113.0/24 to the withdrawn routes, an on-subnet NEXT_HOP is delivered. 'the error SHOULD be logged' (the c14 gap, D-8 fixed): session_next_hop.go::logIgnoredNextHopRoute, called in session_read.go processMessage at the invalid-next-hop branch with the Section 6.3 quote above it, writes 'route ignored: semantically incorrect NEXT_HOP' at Warn, the slogutil default level; the new unit captures the session logger at slog.LevelWarn and asserts level=WARN, peer=192.0.2.1 and next-hop=<the NEXT_HOP> for both invalid cases, and no such record for the valid one. Records: revert on logIgnoredNextHopRoute (+) and invalidReceiveNextHop (-); the author's c15-63red shows the new unit red before the fix. Residual, not a verdict defect: there is NO rate limiting (Ze has none to reuse), so a peer repeating such routes writes one WARN per UPDATE; the RFC asks only that the error be logged. The rib unit TestRFC4271SelfNextHopRouteIsNotInstalled still has no record (unproven). The preceding original note is retained verbatim as historical context; its final proof-status sentence describes that earlier audit. The rib unit now supplies connected terminal routes to the original self next hop 198.51.100.1 and sound next hop 192.168.1.1, so missing reachability cannot substitute for self rejection. Its original assertions remain: the self route produces no best-path change and is absent from the Loc-RIB, while the sound control installs with its original next hop. Its RFC4271-6.3-2 positive claim now states only that observed decision-process exclusion; it makes no claim to observe NOTIFICATION output. The full existing five-tag census is preserved: that rib positive tag and the positive and negative tags of each reactor unit named above. Both reactor units, their logging-and-ignore assertions and valid controls are unchanged. This maintenance judgment adds no claimed discrimination observation; current proof status is established only by the separate discrimination records.

### [`RFC4271-3.1-2`](#rfc4271-3.1-2)

The next hop for each of these routes MUST be resolvable via the local BGP speaker's Routing Table. (§3.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), stale-unit: internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib_aigp.go::RIBManager.reselectAIGPRoutes, internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib_bestchange.go::RIBManager.checkRouteBestChange, internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib_commands.go::RIBManager.gatherPrefixCandidatesForLocked, internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib_next_hop.go::RIBManager.candidateNextHopResolved, internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib_next_hop.go::remoteRIB.prospectiveDistance, internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib_remote.go::RIBManager.igpDistance, internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib_structured.go::RIBManager.handleReceivedStructured, internal/core/rib/locrib/entry.go::selectBest, internal/test/fixture/rfc4271_next_hop_daemon_fixture.go, internal/test/fixture/rfc4271_prospective_readers.go, pkg/plugin/sdk/sdk_engine.go::Plugin.RouteInstall moved. RFC 4271 Section 3.2 requires every Loc-RIB route's next hop to be resolvable through the local Routing Table. Independently read the six tagged CI covers, both real TCP ingress scenarios, their selection/Loc-RIB/kernel observers and the actual admission-to-installation producers. The ordinary internal and forked carriers prove that an unresolved higher-LOCAL_PREF input cannot displace a resolved zero-cost alternate, that loss of both covers removes the retained subject from BGP best, Loc-RIB and every Linux routing table, and that a direct BGP control survives. Restoration uses retained input without another UPDATE. The prospective carriers first install three genuine BGP seeds, establish ordinary resolution of each replacement's next hop, then require withdrawal and absence of the direct and indirectly self-capturing subjects while preserving exact received attributes. The longer interior host route independently resolves the protected replacement, which must remain selected and installed alongside the direct control. Both prospective carriers completed 80 distinct passing runs with all 1120 verification steps each. Read all six accepted 3.1-2 clean/fault receipts and the current native freshness report: all six covers are fresh, with no stale or unproven cover. The ordinary admission-bypass faults fail the initial subject election at 150/24 via unresolved 10.250.1.1; the current-only prospective faults fail the first direct 152/24 forbidden best publication; the over-rejecting containment faults lose protected 154/24 while 151/24 remains visible. The early best-publication failures also violate this Loc-RIB requirement by source coupling: checkRouteBestChange mirrors the selected path before its caller publishes the best change; internal insertion is synchronous, and forked insertion flushes RouteInstall through applyRouteInstall into engine Loc-RIB before returning. With the actual default import configuration, Path.Valid and admit do not add a reachability gate, and upsert retains the selected BGP contribution. FIB rejection cannot repair that Loc-RIB admission. The forked diagnostics show downstream unreachable-route installation before the intended observer failure; route-remove/connection warnings follow failure during shutdown and are not substituted for the semantic failure. This inference is bounded to successful ordinary installation transport and the tested default configuration, not arbitrary RPC failure or rejecting import policy. No direct later Loc-RIB/kernel assertion failure, separately broken indirect case, or separately broken loss/restoration outcome is claimed: fatal primary assertions stop those broken runs. The clean lifecycle assertions and exact protected/direct controls remain mandatory. The bound helper and coupling inventory prevents a later observer, ordering or admission change from retaining this assurance unnoticed.

### [`RFC4271-5.1.2-2`](#rfc4271-5.1.2-2)

a) When a given BGP speaker advertises the route to an internal peer, the advertising speaker SHALL NOT modify the AS_PATH attribute associated with the route. (§5.1.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), stale-unit: internal/component/bgp/reactor/filter_ordered.go::Reactor.runEgressPolicyChain, internal/component/bgp/reactor/filter_ordered.go::Reactor.runEgressPolicyChainASN4, internal/component/bgp/reactor/reactor_api_forward.go::reactorAPIAdapter.forwardUpdateSection moved. Independent re-judgment against RFC 4271 Section 5.1.2(a) ('When a given BGP speaker advertises the route to an internal peer, the advertising speaker SHALL NOT modify the AS_PATH attribute associated with the route.') after RFC 8893 edits to Reactor.runEgressPolicyChainASN4 (filter_ordered.go) and Peer.buildForwardFacts (peer_forward_facts.go). Inspected git diff: runEgressPolicyChainASN4 adds rpkiExport := r.holdsRPKIValidation(library), sets skipRPKIValidation: rpkiExport on libraryIntentContext while keeping asPathEditable: !destInternal unchanged, and calls runPostEgressRPKIValidation only when rpkiExport is true (evaluating read-only rpki-validation-state match members, never modifying AS_PATH toward internal peers). Peer.buildForwardFacts adds two read-only metadata copies (facts.filterInfo.ASOverride = s.ASOverride and facts.filterInfo.SecondaryAS = facts.secondaryAS) without altering isEBGP/IsIBGP classification, AS_PATH preservation toward internal peers, or AS_PATH prepending toward external peers. All 14 tagged tests are unchanged and PASS (go test -tags ze_bgp ./internal/component/bgp/reactor), so the enforced verdict stands.

### [`RFC4271-5.1.2-3`](#rfc4271-5.1.2-3)

When a given BGP speaker advertises the route to an external peer, the advertising speaker updates the AS_PATH attribute as follows: 1) if the first path segment of the AS_PATH is of type AS_SEQUENCE, the local system prepends its own AS number as the last element of the sequence (put it in the leftmost position with respect to the position of octets in the protocol message). If the act of prepending will cause an overflow in the AS_PATH segment (i.e., more than 255 ASes), it SHOULD prepend a new segment of type AS_SEQUENCE and prepend its own AS number to this new segment. 2) if the first path segment of the AS_PATH is of type AS_SET, the local system prepends a new path segment of type AS_SEQUENCE to the AS_PATH, including its own AS number in that segment. 3) if the AS_PATH is empty, the local system creates a path segment of type AS_SEQUENCE, places its own AS into that segment, and places that segment into the AS_PATH. (§5.1.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Independent source rejudgment against committed source b4215935acd576856c4ddfb9160d1d67a6860fd8 after concurrent commit cd0b42213c added TestForwardWithdrawalDoesNotCreateASPath and its native discrimination record. No unrelated working-tree checker edits were included. RFC 4271 Section 5.1.2(b): "When a given BGP speaker advertises the route to an external peer, the advertising speaker updates the AS_PATH attribute as follows:" The following three cases require a local-AS prepend to a leading AS_SEQUENCE, a new leading AS_SEQUENCE before AS_SET, or creation of an AS_SEQUENCE for an empty path, with a new segment recommended on overflow. Section 4.3 separately states: "An UPDATE message might advertise only routes that are to be withdrawn from service, in which case the message will not include path attributes or Network Layer Reachability Information." The existing positive tests cover the actual established announce path with an explicit leading sequence; the ASPathEdit leading-AS_SET and full-255-member sequence branches; and present zero-length AS_PATH through cached and route-server forwarding at both negotiated ASN widths. TestForwardPresentEmptyASPath requires exactly one output, unchanged NLRI and the exact one-member local-AS sequence for an ordinary external recipient, with internal/route-server controls and received-input immutability. TestASPathSlotPrependOnlyWhenAdvertising uses the same prepend intent for advertisement and withdrawal, requires the precise prepended AS-number sequence for the former and no AS_PATH operation for attribute-free withdrawal or MP_UNREACH-only input. The established iBGP control keeps the decoded AS-number list unchanged. Existing limits remain: that iBGP control compares decoded numbers rather than exact TLV bytes, and the overflow test checks the new local-AS segment and retained-segment member count rather than every retained member value. The unchanged checkRelayWithdrawalShape adds independent FRR decoded-path, withdrawal-decode, no-attribute-error and live-session observations, with prior route arrival preceding absence; no fresh interop run is claimed here. The added cd0b42213c test strengthens the negative at actual ForwardUpdate dispatch: forwardOneBody captures exactly one rebuilt raw body from one selected destination; the withdrawal subtest requires absent AS_PATH, complete byte equality and unchanged source input. Its paired advertisement control compares the complete expected UPDATE body, including local AS65000 before source AS65001, next-hop-self192.0.2.1 and unchanged NLRI, and independently checks source immutability. A blanket disabled prepend or next-hop rewrite therefore cannot satisfy that control. The test inspects the dispatched body; this judgment does not extend it into an additional socket-delivery claim. Producing paths were read: ASPathEdit.Record sends non-advertising input to recordWithdrawOnly; recordPrepend/tryShift and ASPath.Prepend implement the sequence, set and empty-path rewrites; forward_build.planAttr independently refuses creation of an absent attribute when advertiseGate is false. The new paired mutation names exactly the two guards that would otherwise mask each other, while each standalone guard can protect the withdrawal. cd0b42213c changes no production source or previous assertion: it adds the bounded negative carrier and proof metadata. The row remains enforced with the stated existing limits. This is source rejudgment only; no new test, guest, paired-mutation, solo-mutation or interop observation is claimed.

### [`RFC4271-5.1.4-3`](#rfc4271-5.1.4-3)

If a BGP speaker is configured to alter the value of the MULTI_EXIT_DISC attribute received over EBGP, then altering the value MUST be done prior to determining the degree of preference of the route and prior to performing route selection (Decision Process phases 1 and 2). (§5.1.4)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), stale-unit: internal/component/bgp/reactor/filter_library.go::Reactor.evaluateLibraryChain, internal/component/bgp/reactor/filter_library.go::Reactor.runLibraryChain, internal/component/bgp/reactor/filter_ordered.go::Reactor.runIngressPolicyChainMeta moved. Independent re-judgment against RFC 4271 Section 5.1.4 ('If a BGP speaker is configured to alter the value of the MULTI_EXIT_DISC attribute received over EBGP, then altering the value MUST be done prior to determining the degree of preference of the route and prior to performing route selection (Decision Process phases 1 and 2).') after RFC 8893 edit to Reactor.evaluateLibraryChain (filter_library.go). Inspected git diff: evaluateLibraryChain adds a single field propagation line copying SkipRPKIValidation and JudgeRPKIValidation from libraryIntentContext onto policychain.ExternalRoute. On the ingress rail (runIngressPolicyChainMeta), both flags are always false, so ingress chain evaluation, MED rewrite via rewriteLibrary/buildModifiedPayload/filter_delta_handlers.go, and pre-decision-process ordering are completely unchanged. Both tagged tests (TestRFC4271ReceivedMEDAlteredBeforeTheDecisionProcess and TestRFC4271MEDAlterationHappensAtIngress) are unchanged and PASS (go test -tags ze_bgp ./internal/component/bgp/reactor). All 23 existing code bindings (including bare filter_delta_handlers.go) and prior discrimination provenance remain valid; the enforced verdict stands.

### [`RFC4271-5.1.5-5`](#rfc4271-5.1.5-5)

A BGP speaker SHALL calculate the degree of preference for each external route based on the locally-configured policy, and include the degree of preference when advertising a route to its internal peers. (§5.1.5)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), stale-unit: internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rfc4271_test.go::TestRFC4271DegreeOfPreferenceNotAHardcodedConstant, internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rfc4271_test.go::TestRFC4271ExternalRouteDegreeOfPreferenceFromLocalPolicy moved. Re-judged 2026-10-31 by an independent auditor who authored none of the change. RFC text (rfc4271): "A BGP speaker SHALL calculate the degree of preference for each external route based on the locally-configured policy, and include the degree of preference when advertising a route to its internal peers." be95c10053 gave receiveExternalRoute an explicit import policy in both cases (FinalAccept, no refs, plus the set-local-pref ref in the policy case) and reworded the negative tag from 'no import policy' to 'an import policy that sets none'; faf7c5c380 renamed filterapi to routepolicy in the same fixture. Under RFC 8212 a route with no import policy is ineligible and is never advertised, so the old negative input no longer reaches the decision; the reworded input keeps its point (nothing on the route names a degree of preference) and the assertions are unchanged: dispatched to the RIB with no LOCAL_PREF (never 200), advertised internally with the default 100. The tag now states the input the body drives. Verdict unchanged: enforced. Earlier judgements are in this file's git history.

### [`RFC4271-5.1.7-1`](#rfc4271-5.1.7-1)

A BGP speaker that performs route aggregation MAY add the AGGREGATOR attribute, which SHALL contain its own AS number and IP address. (§5.1.7)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Re-judged against RFC 4271 Section 5.1.7: "A BGP speaker that performs route aggregation MAY add the AGGREGATOR attribute, which SHALL contain its own AS number and IP address." On the RIB side, aggregateOwnAggregator and TestRFC4271RouteAggregationNextHopAtomicAndAggregator prove that aggregator=true emits speaker-own (65000, 198.51.100.1) — distinct from localIFv4 (198.51.100.10) and contributor AGGREGATORs — on BestChange.Attributes (length 8) and dispatched update-text commands, while aggregator=false omits AGGREGATOR and missing speaker local-as/router-id suppresses rather than forging a bogus AGGREGATOR. On the reactor wire side, planBatchAttrs (reactor_api_batch.go:1767-1795) handles both Builder-backed (batch.Attrs) and wire-backed `update text` (batch.Wire with APIContextID, srcASN4=true) aggregates, and TestRFC4271AggregateWireEncodingFor2Byte4ByteEBGPAndIPv6 verifies both subtests produce 8-byte AGGREGATOR toward 4-byte peers, 6-byte AGGREGATOR (no AS4_AGGREGATOR) toward 2-byte peers for 16-bit ASNs, and 6-byte AGGREGATOR (AS_TRANS) + 8-byte AS4_AGGREGATOR toward 2-byte peers for 32-bit ASNs > 65535. rib.go:1603,1711 also replays active aggregates to ordinary peers on peer-up. All three discrimination records in rfc/discrimination/rfc4271/RFC4271-5.1.7-1.json (RIB positive/negative on aggregateOwnAggregator, reactor positive on reactorAPIAdapter.planBatchAttrs) are current (producer-disable revert records).

### [`RFC4271-6.7-4`](#rfc4271-6.7-4)

If the BGP speaker decides to terminate its BGP connection with a neighbor because the number of address prefixes received from the neighbor exceeds the locally-configured, upper bound, then the speaker MUST send the neighbor a NOTIFICATION message with the Error Code Cease. (§6.7)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Peer level: running Peer Established with ipv4/unicast maximum 2 and teardown default, far end announces 3 prefixes -> exactly one NOTIFICATION 6/1 (Cease, Maximum Number of Prefixes Reached) read off the socket, then EOF (TestRFC4271PrefixLimitTeardownSendsCease). Negative: teardown false, same 3 prefixes -> no NOTIFICATION in 500 ms, connection up, Established, so the Cease is bound to the decision to terminate. checkPrefixLimits break observed red both polarities. Closes 'the send to the neighbor is not exercised'. HEAD units TestPrefixExceedTeardown/TestPrefixExceedDrop stay supplementary (return-value level). Re-read 2026-10-01 (c10 judge): the only change under this verdict is a tag comment line for another id (RFC4271-6.7-1 / RFC4271-Security-1 added, RFC4271-9.1.2.1-1 removed) in the unit's doc comment; every assertion is byte-identical, so the judgement stands.

### [`RFC4271-6.8-2`](#rfc4271-6.8-2)

Upon receipt of an OPEN message, the local system MUST examine all of its connections that are in the OpenConfirm state. (§6.8)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Re-judged 2026-10-01 (BGP c12 judge). The trigger (receipt of an OPEN) now reaches the examination in a tagged unit: TestRFC4271ReceivedOpenExaminesTheOpenConfirmConnection hands a second connection to Reactor.acceptOrReject, which tracks it as pending until its OPEN, then writes a complete OPEN on it; handlePendingCollision examines the session held in OpenConfirm. Both outcomes of the examination are asserted on the wire: remote Identifier 10.0.0.3 above local 10.0.0.2 -> Cease 6/7 on the OpenConfirm connection; 10.0.0.1 below -> 6/7 on the new connection, pending cleared, OpenConfirm session kept. Negative TestRFC4271ReceivedOpenWithNoOpenConfirmConnection: with no connection in OpenConfirm (session Established) an OPEN with Identifier 255.255.255.255, which would win an OpenConfirm comparison, does not close the existing session; the new connection gets 6/7 and Established stays. Records on session.go::detectCollision observed red both polarities. HEAD units in collision_test.go (direct detectCollision calls) stay supplementary. Ze holds at most one OpenConfirm connection per peer, so 'all' is that one.

### [`RFC4271-9-2`](#rfc4271-9-2)

If the UPDATE message contains a feasible route, the Adj-RIB-In will be updated with this route as follows: if the NLRI of the new route is identical to the one the route currently has stored in the Adj- RIB-In, then the new route SHALL replace the older route in the Adj- RIB-In, thus implicitly withdrawing the older route from service. (§9)

The unchanged storage replacement unit checks newer attributes replace the old attributes; its unrelated-prefix removal negative is supplementary and does not replace the receive pair. ### [`RFC4271-9.1.1-2`](#rfc4271-9.1.1-2)

the return value MUST be used as the LOCAL_PREF value in any IBGP readvertisement. (§9.1.1)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Re-judged 2026-11-06 by an independent auditor who authored none of the change; no test or record ran for this judgement. RFC 4271 Section 9.1.1: "otherwise, the return value MUST be used as the LOCAL_PREF value in any IBGP readvertisement." Bound: the unit drives the general forward rail. The route-server rail shares applyFactsLocalPref (forward_rs.go:553), and the rib plugin's readvertisement is not driven by it. Verdict unchanged: enforced. Earlier judgements are in this file's git history.

### [`RFC4271-9.1.2-1`](#rfc4271-9.1.2-1)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), stale-unit: internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rfc4271_next_hop_reachability_test.go, internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib_aigp.go::RIBManager.reselectAIGPRoutes, internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib_bestchange.go::RIBManager.checkRouteBestChange, internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib_commands.go::RIBManager.gatherPrefixCandidatesForLocked, internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib_next_hop.go::RIBManager.candidateNextHopResolved, internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib_next_hop.go::remoteRIB.prospectiveDistance, internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib_remote.go::RIBManager.igpDistance, internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib_structured.go::RIBManager.handleReceivedStructured, internal/core/rib/locrib/entry.go::selectBest, internal/test/fixture/rfc4271_next_hop_daemon_fixture.go, internal/test/fixture/rfc4271_prospective_readers.go, pkg/plugin/sdk/sdk_engine.go::Plugin.RouteInstall moved. RFC 4271 Section 9.1.2 requires exclusion from Phase 2 both when a next hop is currently unresolved and when installing the candidate would make it unresolved. Independently re-read all seven tagged admission roots and all four daemon carriers, their fixtures and the producing gather, ordinary/prospective resolution, selection and mirroring paths. The units use real received-path storage and Loc-RIB resolution to assert exclusion of an unresolved preference, a lone unresolved route, existing self/mutual recursion and prospective direct/indirect recursion. Genuine feasible controls assert exact admission of zero cost, terminating independent recursion, longer-prefix protection and a same-prefix connected winner. The old live carriers retain their complete real TCP ingress, BGP best, Loc-RIB and exact Linux installation/loss/recovery assertions, with reachable alternate and direct control. The new internal and forked carriers close the former live prospective gap: they install seeds, prove ordinary resolution before each replacement, retain exact wire attributes, reject direct and indirect self-capture, require absence from BGP best/Loc-RIB/every kernel table, and preserve the longer-host-route protected replacement and independent direct control. Authenticated process placement and real all-table kernel queries prevent an in-process surrogate or empty/error query from satisfying these assertions. Both prospective carriers completed 80 distinct passing runs and 1120 verification steps each. Read the four accepted new 9.1.2-1 clean/fault receipts and the current native freshness report retaining all eleven older records: all fifteen covers are fresh, with no stale or unproven cover. In each placement the current-only fault produces the exact forbidden direct 152/24 via 152.1 best publication; the containment fault loses protected 154/24 while the independent 151/24 direct control remains visible. These isolate prospective admission and over-rejection rather than transport failure. The live positive fault stops at the first direct case, so no separately observed indirect live RED or later broken Loc-RIB/kernel assertion is claimed. The negative fault stops at protected best selection before its later Loc-RIB/kernel checks. Clean executions still assert the complete direct/indirect and protected outcomes. Retained unit discrimination of longer-prefix protection does not separately discriminate the independent-recursive leaf; retained old live faults fail initial unresolved or zero-cost selection and do not separately break later restoration. Those limits do not erase the exact clean assertions. The complete bound fixture/helper and production inventory preserves the meaning of all fifteen covers. This is the full default forwarding-admission requirement; it does not remove the explicit source-owned non-forwarding Adj-RIB-Out recovery view or assert arbitrary RPC-failure behavior.

Same-scope independent rejudgment of the complete RFC 4271 Section 9.1.2 sentence: a change of either the immediate next hop or the IGP cost to NEXT_HOP requires Phase 2 selection again. Read all six tagged covers, their actual unit and daemon fixtures, the production Loc-RIB subscriber, forked metric revision/cache worker, extraction, ranking and mirroring paths, the retained record cuts and the renewed negative receipt. The immediate-hop unit drains initial and no-op extraction observations before changing only the interior gateway at fixed cost 10. It requires a new production candidate lookup against that gateway, resolution true, unchanged received BGP NEXT_HOP and no AIGP. The positive fault removes immediate-hop change detection in Path.Equal. The renewed negative fault corrupts only the mirrored received NEXT_HOP; its accepted clean run passes, while its observed mismatch is the initial learned-address preservation assertion at line 101. That is an honest preservation control, not a separately observed post-change failure. The cost-only unit isolates equal earlier BGP attributes without AIGP, arranges the cheaper route against the later address tie-breaker, changes only interior cost A10 to A30 to A5, and requires selected Loc-RIB A to B to A without another UPDATE or a direct selector call. Its retained subscriber cut preserves initial selection and fails the first required cost-driven move to B. The retained-input unit additionally asserts initial preference, reachable fallback after one cover disappears, absence after both disappear, retention of received paths and restoration without UPDATE. Its selection-trigger cut and each ordinary internal/forked CI selection-trigger cut preserve initial selection and fail after interior-cover change. Both real-socket daemon placements retain their unchanged BGP-best, Loc-RIB, exact all-table kernel and direct-control lifecycle assertions; authenticated placement and successful queries are required. Forked revision changes clear ordinary and prospective caches before retained-route reselection. These carriers do not separately prove cost-only or immediate-gateway-only daemon/kernel behavior. The old CIs gained only an additional 3.1-2 tag; no old tag, input, assertion or lifecycle stage was removed. Read the accepted renewed initial-preservation diagnostic and independently parsed the final native freshness report: all six covers are current, with zero stale and zero unproven records. Exact clean assertions cover both mandatory triggers and the feasible preservation control; no assertion or claim was weakened to renew the record. A discrimination record is confined to its observed primary failure, not proof that every later recovery assertion ran under its fault. The bound production and fixture/helper inventory preserves those meanings. No broader ranking, packet-forwarding or new prospective-integration claim is made by this rejudgment.

### [`RFC4271-9.1.2.1-1`](#rfc4271-9.1.2.1-1)

Notice that even though BGP routes do not have to be installed in the Routing Table with the immediate next-hop(s), implementations MUST take care that, before any packets are forwarded along a BGP route, its associated NEXT_HOP address is resolved to the immediate (directly connected) next-hop address, and that this address (or multiple addresses) is finally used for actual packet forwarding. (§9.1.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Re-judged 2026-10-01 (c10 judge). The RIB-level tags (which proved the Loc-RIB copies NEXT_HOP unresolved, RFC4271-9.1.2-3) are gone; resolution is proven where Ze performs it, in sysrib. Positive TestRFC4271BGPNextHopResolvedToTheImmediateNextHop: BGP /24 NEXT_HOP 172.16.5.5 inside an IGP /16 via 10.0.0.1 inside a connected /24 on eth1; the (system-rib, best-change) Add the FIB writer programs carries 10.0.0.1 + eth1, protocol bgp, and no entry ever carries 172.16.5.5 (resolveMember, record observed red). Negative TestRFC4271BGPRouteWithAnUnresolvedNextHopLeavesTheFIB: the IGP /16 is removed, a Withdraw is published and only removes follow for 200 ms (cascadeRecompute, record observed red). Caveat: a BGP route whose NEXT_HOP was NEVER resolvable in the Loc-RIB is published with its raw gateway (fibEntry fibUnproved); the Linux FIB refuses an off-link gateway, so no packet is forwarded along it, but no unit asserts that kernel refusal.

Explicit gateway/device fixture routes still take that path; suppression of otherwise valid targetless output does not substitute for withdrawal. The old CIs gained only an additional 3.1-2 comment and preserve every old tag and assertion. Independently parsed the final native freshness receipt: all six covers are fresh, zero stale and zero unproven, including the two renewed current checkRouteBestChange records. Both removal destinations and feasible fallback/preservation are enforced in this bounded IPv4 internal/forked topology. Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), stale-unit: internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rfc4271_bgp_identifier_json_test.go::TestBestPathEqualBGPIdentifiersOnTheJSONRailFallThroughToPeerAddress, internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rfc4271_bgp_identifier_json_test.go::TestBestPathStepFComparesThePeerBGPIdentifierOnTheJSONRail, internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rfc4271_bgp_identifier_test.go::TestBestPathEqualBGPIdentifiersFallThroughToPeerAddress, internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rfc4271_bgp_identifier_test.go::TestBestPathStepFComparesThePeerBGPIdentifier, internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rfc4271_whole_set_med_test.go::TestRFC4271WholeSetMEDRIBBestChange moved. Independent rejudgment 2026-11-15 of the unchanged requirement and all ten tagged functions after four received-identifier fixture topology additions and the whole-set RIB consumer topology repair. RFC 4271 Section 9.1.2.2 states: 'The criteria MUST be applied in the order specified.' The full criteria (a)-(g), including whole-set elimination and MED comparability only within the neighboring AS, were read. Section 9.1.2 first excludes an unresolvable NEXT_HOP. Each structured and production-JSON identifier test now supplies only an independent connected 10.0.0.1/32 route, with real prospective Loc-RIB resolution and cost zero. UPDATE bytes, two candidates, peer addresses and BGP Identifiers are unchanged. The positive pair makes step f choose the higher-address peer with the lower BGP Identifier; the negative pair ties identifiers and requires step g to choose the lower peer address. These tests exercise metadata extraction through actual received events and assert exact comparison steps and winners. The topology adds no admission bypass and leaves unknown-next-hop controls unchanged. The unchanged adjacent-criteria test conflicts each earlier criterion with the next in both argument orders. The unchanged whole-set MED candidate tests cover all six permutations, require C to lose to A at MED before B beats A at Router ID, and forbid an earlier-criteria loser from eliminating C at MED. FullTiebreak and same-neighbor MED remain supplementary; the latter also prevents a different neighboring AS from winning through its lower MED. The selection producer removes earlier-criteria losers before grouping same-neighbor MED survivors and comparing later criteria. TestRFC4271WholeSetMEDRIBBestChange now supplies only independent connected routes to its unchanged next hops 192.0.2.3, 192.0.2.2 and 192.0.2.1. Its six permutations still exercise actual received attributes, exact Loc-RIB B-to-C-to-B selection as non-best MED witness A leaves and returns, exact replacement events and no spurious unchanged publication. Main's b6e1a9ef baseline passed this consumer with race (1.650s); the earlier 4d7e8db1 selected repaired RIB roots and peer regression passed with race and no skips. I inspected all five new RFC4271 normal-return semantic proof logs and their current native records. In d5b651a5 and 28d0bd7d, reversing compareAfterMED's Router ID comparison first passed the named structured or JSON baseline, then failed its exact winner assertion: expected 192.0.2.2, actual 192.0.2.1. In 147c6181 and c7ad240b, reversing the peer-address comparison first passed each equal-Identifier baseline, then failed its exact winner assertion: expected 192.0.2.1, actual 192.0.2.2. In 3c3ec480, replacing retainLowestMED's selected survivor count with the full eligible count first passed the whole-set RIB consumer baseline, then failed the first Loc-RIB winner assertion: expected B through 192.0.2.2, actual A through 192.0.2.1. The observed winner was A, not the proposal's predicted C; the actual incorrect winner is the evidence. These five native mutant records bind the changed unit bodies and each precise claim. They show ordinary assertion failures, not panics, skips or build failures, and do not extend proof to other breaks. Other tagged units, requirement quotes, levels and discrimination entries are unchanged. This pending file does not itself stamp or publish an audit.

Audit verdict: not reviewed: no reviewer has checked whether these tests enforce the requirement

No test carries RFC4271-9.1.2.2-2, so there is no linked test.

### [`RFC4271-9.1.2.2-3`](#rfc4271-9.1.2.2-3)

For IBGP- learned routes, the MULTI_EXIT_DISC MUST be used in route comparisons that reach this step in the Decision Process. (§9.1.2.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Re-judged 2026-10-01 (BGP c13 judge). The MISSING clause (b) of Section 9.1.2.2 (c) is closed at the producer: firstASInPath answers 0 for a path that begins with an AS_SET (quote above the statement), and neighborAS turns 0 into the local AS for an IBGP route (PeerASN == LocalASN). Positive TestRFC4271IBGPAggregatesLedByASSetsCompareMED: two IBGP aggregates led by different AS_SETs share neighbor AS = local AS, MED 10 beats MED 20 at BestStepMED against IGP cost 50 vs 5, and SelectBest agrees. Negative TestRFC4271IBGPRouteLedByASSequenceKeepsItsNeighborAS: an AS_SEQUENCE-led IBGP route keeps neighbor AS 65010 against an aggregate led by AS_SET {65010, 65020}, MED is skipped and the IGP cost decides, so the local-AS rule is not over-applied. Both build candidates from wire bytes through firstASInPath and asPathLength, the calls extractCandidate (rib_commands.go) makes. Their records break the producer with a panic (reach only); a judge overlay (tree untouched) removing only the AS_SET check reddens both units and TestFirstASInPath, whose wrong expectation (65003, the set's first member) was corrected to 0 under D-15, so the clause is discriminated. HEAD units unchanged: empty-AS_PATH IBGP positive and the later-steps negative (2026-09-30 judge mutants). Side effect read: an EBGP route led by an AS_SET now compares no MED (route-selection.md); such a path breaks Section 5.1.2 b) at its sender, and MED is owed only between routes of one known neighbor AS, so not comparing it stays conformant. Residual outside this row's RFC 4271 reading: the RIB classifies IBGP by PeerASN == LocalASN, not the reactor's RFC 7705-aware rule (journal helper-bypassed-by-an-open-coded-copy.md).
