### [`RFC4271-9.2-2`](#rfc4271-9.2-2)

A route SHALL NOT be installed in the Adj-Rib-Out unless the destination, and NEXT_HOP described by this route, may be forwarded appropriately by the Routing Table. (§9.1.3)

Records cover only part of this requirement: tested "may be forwarded appropriately by the Routing Table"; gap "installed in the Adj-Rib-Out"; the ordinary IPv4 internal-plugin witnesses check sampled destination and emitted NEXT_HOP resolvability at stable successful recipient writes, initial unresolved-route exclusion, reachability loss/recovery and replacement-recipient replay. The remaining stable successful-write oracle is unproven across forked prospective resolution in internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib_next_hop.go::remoteRIB.prospectiveDistance, Routing-Table revision invalidation in internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib_remote.go::RIBManager.runRemoteMetrics, and remote route publication. Earlier competing received-route replay advertised retained unresolved input to a replacement Session; the ordinary-replay-owned guard now stands that auxiliary replay down, and TestRFC4271OrdinaryReplacementRecipientSkipsUnresolvedRoute has a recorded structured-guard negative at its observed source version. Fresh native retakes observed the changed mirror omission producing successful unforwardable destination output and the structured replay-guard omission leaking retained unresolved input to a useful replacement recipient; the unchanged local prospective-admission negative remains a separate recorded cover. These are proof limits, not demonstrated absence of the remote behavior. Section 9.1.3's explicit local-policy exception remains supported and is not exercised here.; they do not verify the whole requirement.

Audit verdict: partial (the tests enforce only the declared tested scope; the whole requirement remains unmet), stale-unit: internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib_bestchange.go::RIBManager.checkRouteBestChange, internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib_next_hop.go::RIBManager.candidateNextHopResolved, internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib_next_hop.go::remoteRIB.prospectiveDistance moved. Independent current-source rejudgment: retain PARTIAL under RFC 4271 Section 9.1.3. Tested is "may be forwarded appropriately by the Routing Table"; Gap is "installed in the Adj-Rib-Out". The genuine remaining gap-context Producer is internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib_next_hop.go::remoteRIB.prospectiveDistance. The forked prospective-resolution RPC/cache, Routing-Table revision invalidation in internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib_remote.go::RIBManager.runRemoteMetrics, and remote insert/remove publication are implemented; the stable successful-write destination and emitted-NEXT_HOP oracle has not been demonstrated across those forked boundaries. This is a testing limit, not demonstrated absence of remote behavior, and earns zero whole-requirement credit. Section 9.1.3's local-policy exception remains outside these default-policy witnesses.

Independently reread both complete current roots, their framing/write/revision/history and settlement helpers, actual local and remote candidate/publication producers and structured replay guard, and the RFC's full Phase 3 context. TestRFC4271OrdinaryOutputRequiresForwardableRoute has distinct positive and negative tags. Its independently resolvable control must reach the actual ordinary internal recipient; initially unresolved retained input must yield zero announcements and no destination cover after ingress, plugin-event, forwarding and capture settlement. Reachability insertion, loss withdrawal and restoration then require useful recovery without another source UPDATE, preserving attributes and control output. The observer samples each subject/control destination and actual emitted NEXT_HOP under one stable Routing-Table revision around a complete successful transport write, independently pairing observations with captured announcement history. Framing errors, short writes, crossed revisions, lost history and bounded-observer overflow fail the fixture. This is neither a kernel-forwarding acknowledgment nor a blanket family/platform claim.

TestRFC4271OrdinaryReplacementRecipientSkipsUnresolvedRoute establishes useful original P/K, removes P's reachability, requires its service withdrawal while retained received input survives, closes and joins the original capture, then requires an actual distinct recipient Session and nonzero different destination token with the same selected owner. Useful K must reach the replacement while unresolved retained P has zero announcements; reachability restoration recovers P with no new source input. The structured ordinary-replay guard is now a tested auxiliary-replay exclusion boundary, not the remaining remote gap.

Fresh actual native job-advertiser-rfc4271-forwardability-retirement-reproof-d7e5742a.log: positive clean PASS at line10, mutant record at40. Omitting only ordinary CIDR mirroring in checkRouteBestChange makes the first nonfatal assertion at source line99 identify a successful control announcement at Routing-Table revision1 whose destination is unresolved while its actual NEXT_HOP remains independently resolved. Later subject, withdrawal and history assertions also fail; there is no fatal setup or revision-indeterminate failure and the lifecycle/cleanup execute. This renews the positive destination-forwardability oracle at the current recorded producer. Replacement negative clean PASS at82, mutant record at106. Omitting only se.OrdinaryReplayOwned in AdjRIBInManager.handleStructuredState allows retained unresolved P to escape after useful original output, loss withdrawal, old-session join and useful replacement K. The first failures at source lines305/307 identify the unexpected retained route and exactly one unresolved replacement announcement, followed by stable successful-write destination/NEXT_HOP failures. The recovery and cleanup also execute. Neither native record claims a failure in an unrelated neighboring policy rule.

The separate unchanged local prospective-admission negative remains recorded: candidateNextHopResolved's normal-return admission bypass was previously observed in job-rfc4271-forwardability-unresolved-proof-a0fd9833.log after a clean baseline; the initial forbidden subject announcement fails nonfatally, then the unexpected destination cover fails fatally, and cleanup identifies the comparable unresolved successful write. Later skipped body assertions are not claimed. Its canonical record retains the current enclosing-root unit fingerprint shared with the fresh positive and its original distinct negative claim/producer fingerprint; native partial stamping validates all three current claims. The fresh changed-producer receipts do not replace this distinct witness. The third attempted RFC4271-9.2-3 cut in the combined native job stayed green and wrote nothing; it provides no evidence for this row and is not credited here. Conceptual-table equivalence under RFC4271 Sections3.2/9.1 does not invent a physical Adj-RIB-Out object as an extra obligation; the default internal IPv4 lifecycle and replacement are the bounded proven scope, and remote-output proof remains open.

### [`RFC4271-9.2-4`](#rfc4271-9.2-4)

If a BGP speaker receives overlapping routes, the Decision Process MUST consider both routes based on the configured acceptance policy. (§9.1.4)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), stale-unit: internal/component/bgp/reactor/reactor_notify.go::Reactor.notifyMessageReceiverFrom, internal/component/bgp/reactor/reactor_notify.go::Reactor.notifyOneMessage moved. Independently re-read the full RFC 4271 Section 9.1.4 obligation: "If a BGP speaker receives overlapping routes, the Decision Process MUST consider both routes based on the configured acceptance policy." The receive positive requires both accepted /8 and /24 in Loc-RIB and exactly two adds; the receive negative checks both arrival orders, no withdrawal, the earlier route still present and two Adj-RIB-In entries. Both preserve the original UPDATE bytes and use the repaired real connected next-hop prerequisites. The unchanged reactor pair supplies the acceptance-policy boundary: registered ASN4 WireUpdate context permits the real policy-chain prefix read; the positive asks the chain twice and dispatches both NLRI in order, while the negative rejects the /24 and dispatches exactly the accepted /8. Its answer transport supplies the filter verdict rather than a parsed filter configuration, as in the existing claim. All four tagged carriers, the existing enforced verdict, MUST level and quoted requirement scope are retained. Main native receive refresh 32c5b989 recorded both existing receive covers with a named clean baseline PASS followed by intentional producer-disable panic red through checkRouteBestChange. Main native policy refresh 744678ce recorded both existing reactor covers with named clean baseline PASS followed by intentional producer-disable panic red: negative Reactor.runIngressPolicyChainMeta producer SHA 1434aa47d5cd0c08 and positive Reactor.notifyMessageReceiver SHA e77b6eb3fba4d9d7. Each retains its original unit, polarity, revert route and producer; no record was added or removed. These are observed producer-disable checks, not normal-return semantic-mutant proof or upgraded credit. The two policy records name the frozen Atomic working-tree producer bodies inspected for this judgment; those source changes were not yet in HEAD. Record and audit publication must be coordinated with Atomic's producer commit so the published dependency matches those observed bodies. Independent moved-producer source rejudgment: reread RFC 4271 Section 9.1.4, all four tagged carriers and assertion-producing helpers, and the full current notification/ingress/publication path. The reactor policy pair still presents both overlapping NLRI to the real configured library-chain answer transport; accepting both yields both in arrival order and rejecting only the more-specific yields only the accepted less-specific. The separate received-route pair still observes both Loc-RIB installations, no displacement on either arrival order and no withdrawal. notifyMessageReceiver is now a delegate to notifyMessageReceiverFrom, which owns receipt-aware continuations and invokes notifyOneMessage; the latter still applies acceptance policy before caching or consumer publication. Exact configured-Peer input prevents address-only lookup from substituting a different configured peer; legacy direct test callers pass nil and retain their existing bounded semantics. Preserve all four entries, exact MUST quote and enforced verdict. Renew only the existing positive entry-point body-panic record at the moved From function after source freeze, retaining its producer-reachability limitation; no new normal-return mutant or live source-session conformance is inferred. Final same-boundary receipt reconciliation: independently read complete job-closure-rfc-renewal-9-799bf60e.log and the current From/notifyOneMessage producer boundary. The exact positive overlapping-policy carrier passed clean and the intentional From body-panic produced its named red; native recording accepted the original positive cover at the moved function. This supersedes the earlier pending renewal language and retains the producer-reachability limitation, without claiming a new normal-return semantic mutant or rerun of the other three covers. Both named producer functions are wholly in HEAD-equal reactor_notify.go at HEAD 751524b526 after foundation d518d29e1c, so their code fingerprints bind the actual published enforcing source. All four original test entries remain.

### [`RFC4271-9.2.2.2-2`](#rfc4271-9.2.2.2-2)

ORIGIN attribute: If at least one route among routes that are aggregated has ORIGIN with the value INCOMPLETE, then the aggregated route MUST have the ORIGIN attribute with the value INCOMPLETE. Otherwise, if at least one route among routes that are aggregated has ORIGIN with the value EGP, then the aggregated route MUST have the ORIGIN attribute with the value EGP. (§9.2.2.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Re-judged against RFC 4271 Section 9.2.2.2: "ORIGIN attribute: If at least one route among routes that are aggregated has ORIGIN with the value INCOMPLETE, then the aggregated route MUST have the ORIGIN attribute with the value INCOMPLETE. Otherwise, if at least one route among routes that are aggregated has ORIGIN with the value EGP, then the aggregated route MUST have the ORIGIN attribute with the value EGP." aggregateOrigin implements INCOMPLETE > EGP > IGP precedence. TestRFC4271RouteAggregationMEDAndOrigin isolates ORIGIN under fixed MED=50, AS_PATH and NEXT_HOP, verifying {IGP,IGP}->IGP, {IGP,EGP}->EGP, {IGP,INCOMPLETE}->INCOMPLETE on BestChange wire AttrOrigin and dispatched update-text commands, and {EGP,INCOMPLETE}->INCOMPLETE retention on ActiveAggregate. Both positive and negative discrimination records in rfc/discrimination/rfc4271/RFC4271-9.2.2.2-2.json are current (producer-disable revert records).

### [`RFC4271-Security-1`](#rfc4271-security-1)

An implementation MUST support the TCP MD5 option [RFC2385]. (§E)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Collateral source re-judgment 2026-10-02 after unrelated MD5 requirement tags were removed from TestRFC2385MatchingKeysCarryASignedSession's enclosing doc comment. RFC4271 AppendixE states: An implementation MUST support the TCP MD5 option [RFC2385]. Its Security Considerations also require per-peer activation support. Every currently tagged unit was reread: TestRFC2385ConfiguredKeyReachesBothSockets drives an operator password through parsePeerFromTree and NewSession and asserts the exact key and peer address in RealDialer and md5PeersForListener; TestMD5PeersForListener and its second polarity assert two keyed default-port peers, MD5IP override, exclusion of an unkeyed peer and differently bound listener, the separate keyed custom-port peer, and an empty unrelated port. TestRFC2385MatchingKeysCarryASignedSession exercises actual RealDialer/RealListenerFactory sockets with matching keys and requires256KiB received; TestRFC2385KeyOnOneEndOnlyCarriesNoSession requires timeout for an unsigned dial into the keyed listener and fails on a successful connection, discriminating a no-op option installer. Source trace reaches reactor.newListenerFactory, which installs the collected keys in RealListenerFactory, and both socket Control callbacks reach md5_linux.go::setTCPMD5Sig before bind/connect with errors propagated. Configuration-only tests are supplementary, not a substitute for the real sockets. This preserves the Linux support verdict and does not claim macOS MD5 support, exact wire digest composition from the old loopback unit, or any new native execution. Unsupported-kernel skips are not positive evidence; the separately reviewed configured runtime and stock kernel emitted configs now establish TCP_MD5SIG availability. Existing executable assertions and this row's tag claims are unchanged; Main must stamp fresh enclosing-unit hashes with mode rejudge, not treat changed doc comments as mere shifted-file reseals.

In both functions, the added emitPrePolicyAdjRIBOut call runs strictly AFTER the iBGP split-horizon refusal check (isReflected / reflected with neither source nor destination an RR client continues before reaching emitPrePolicyAdjRIBOut), copies the incoming UPDATE payload read-only for BMP pre-policy Adj-RIB-Out observation, and does not alter split-horizon refusal, route-reflector client exceptions, mods, or forwarding dispatch. ### [`RFC4271-9.2-7`](#rfc4271-9.2-7)

All newly installed routes and all newly unfeasible routes for which there is no replacement route SHALL be advertised to its peers by means of an UPDATE message. (§9.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Independently rejudged 2026-11-19 against RFC 4271 Section 9.2 and all three current tagged units. The existing route-server positive first establishes useful sole-source output, then requires exactly one withdrawal, no remaining prefix and no duplicate announcement after that source withdraws. The existing negative first proves useful B-then-better-A output, then requires the retained B attributes and zero bare withdrawals after A withdraws. Both inspect ordered recipient TCP history behind a settled-decision barrier and a subsequent wire fence. Their explicit accepting policies, independently installed terminal next-hop routes and real registered bgp-rs, bgp-rib and bgp-adj-rib-in isolate the withdrawal/replacement condition. The two existing route-server discrimination records remain unchanged: queueRecoveryWithdrawal's error-guard mutation omits the owed withdrawal, while Session.recoveryOwedFor returning false bypasses registered source election; the latter is broader than a withdrawal-only cut, and the useful B/A baselines and later exact replacement assertions bound its claim. The additional positive test/plugin/bgp-best-route-transit-default.ci drives actual daemon configuration with internal and forked bgp-rib, explicit grants, accepting export policy and no route-server. Both original sockets complete EOR and next-hop readiness; useful API output precedes a legitimate iBGP UPDATE. The driver verifies the selected source and requires the exact prefix, ORIGIN and two-octet AS_PATH containing local AS 65000 on the original eBGP recipient socket. Native job-advertiser-rfc4271-ordinary-final-record-b523a90d.log records a clean named PASS in 2.2 seconds, followed by the native revert of selectedDispatch.publish and a named broken failure. The internal placement reaches the elected-route socket assertion and receives no announcement; the forked placement loses its deciding process and fails before election can be observed, so this record does not establish a separate forked final-writer failure. The new tag claims initial ordinary IPv4 announcement only. Later withdrawal and API-survivor assertions remain useful controls but add no retirement-specific discrimination or ordinary replacement-present negative claim. Existing route-server claims and records remain separate and supply the no-replacement withdrawal and replacement-present control; the new ordinary positive adds initial advertisement evidence without changing the requirement quote, annotation or existing polarity pair.

### [`RFC4271-9.2-8`](#rfc4271-9.2-8)

Any routes in the Loc-RIB marked as unfeasible SHALL be removed (§9.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), stale-unit: internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rfc4271_rib_bestchange_test.go::TestLocRIBMirror moved. Independently re-read RFC 4271 Section 9.2: "Any routes in the Loc-RIB marked as unfeasible SHALL be removed." The existing positive TestLocRIBMirror inserts one BGP route through checkBestPathChange, verifies the selected Loc-RIB path with exact eBGP distance and next hop, removes its only Adj-RIB-In route, reruns selection and requires loc.Best to report the prefix absent. Its only repair supplies the real connected 192.168.1.1/32 prerequisite via electionFixtureLocRIB; the no-candidate removal assertions and input attributes are unchanged. Both existing reactor negatives remain in full scope: live bgp-rs/bgp-rib/bgp-adj-rib-in sessions announce A's preferred path, then announce and withdraw B's worse path. The wire carrier requires A's exact path, one announcement and zero withdrawals after settled decision and TCP fences; the Loc-RIB carrier waits until bgp-rib stores B's post-withdrawal marker before requiring A's still-feasible path and exact next hop in Loc-RIB. These are distinct wire and Loc-RIB observations confining removal to infeasible routes. The two existing negative MUTANT records are preserved byte-for-byte; no positive discrimination receipt exists and none is invented. Verdict, requirement quote, level and all tagged scope are unchanged. Independent rejudgment preserves the existing verdict, requirement quote, level and full tagged scope. Main receive baseline 4f70ab4b passed all 11 roots (12 leaf cases), race enabled, without skips; supplementary baseline d3723dd3 passed TestLocRIBMirror and TestRIBSamePrefixInWithdrawnAndNLRIInstallsTheRoute. Main native refresh 32c5b989 completed all 13 existing receive-cover records: each named clean baseline passed and its existing revert route disabled the named producer with an intentional panic, producing the recorded red. Those observations retain their original producer-disable route and provenance; they are not normal-return semantic-mutant proof and do not add credit to unrecorded supplementary carriers. This fixture maintenance changes sibling test setup in rfc4271_rib_bestchange_test.go while the tagged TestLocRIBMirror body and claims remain byte-identical. Main focused streaming baseline b535c0e1 passed that carrier within its ten-root scope, retaining its exact selected-path and post-withdrawal absence checks. Both route-server reactor negative carriers remain in the full three-cover population, and both existing normal-return MUTANT records remain unchanged. This maintenance claims no fresh execution of those reactor carriers and invents no positive discrimination receipt for TestLocRIBMirror; the enforced verdict and all existing limits remain unchanged. Independent companion-fixture rejudgment against RFC 4271 Section 9.2, all three tagged entries and their assertion-producing helpers: TestLocRIBMirror still installs the sole received BGP route, observes its selected path, removes its only Adj-RIB-In candidate and requires Loc-RIB absence. Both distinct negative reactor carriers still retain A's feasible route after B's worse route is announced and withdrawn, observing respectively exact settled client TCP history and Loc-RIB next hop after the source-storage marker barrier. The shared recovery fixture now installs independent terminal interior host routes and asserts next-hop resolution, without inserting any subject BGP prefix or changing the feasibility inputs, withdrawal, marker, retained path or absence assertions. Existing three-entry scope and enforced verdict remain. The prior absence of a positive discrimination receipt for TestLocRIBMirror is not silently filled or newly claimed by this rejudgment.

### [`RFC4271-9.2-9`](#rfc4271-9.2-9)

Changes to the reachable destinations within its own autonomous system SHALL also be advertised in an UPDATE message. (§9.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Re-judged 2026-10-01 (BGP c18 judge). The old objection (units call the encoders only) is closed at the entry point: NEW TestRFC4271OwnASDestinationBecomingReachableIsSentToPeers originates 10.20.0.0/24 through reactorAPIAdapter.AnnounceNLRIBatch (the API announce entry point) toward one internal and one external Established peer and parses each connection's bytes: exactly one UPDATE, Withdrawn Routes empty, attributes present, NLRI = the prefix. NEW TestRFC4271OwnASDestinationBecomingUnreachableIsSentToPeers: after that announcement WithdrawNLRIBatch writes exactly one more UPDATE per peer with the prefix in Withdrawn Routes, no attributes, no NLRI, so a change that removes reachability is advertised, not left standing. Observed-red records: + on reactor_api_batch.go::AnnounceNLRIBatch, - on WithdrawNLRIBatch. The HEAD encoder units (writeAnnounceUpdate/writeWithdrawUpdate) stay as supplementary. Residual, not a clause gap: own-AS reachability changes are driven through the API rail; static-route reload and default-originate rails are not exercised by these units.

If, due to the limits on the maximum size of an UPDATE message (see Section 4), a single route doesn't fit into the message, the BGP speaker MUST not advertise the route to its peers and MAY choose to log an error locally. (§9.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), stale-unit: internal/component/bgp/reactor/rfc4271_forward_oversize_route_test.go::TestRFC4271RelayedRouteTooLargeForThePeerIsWithheld, internal/component/bgp/reactor/rfc4271_forward_oversize_route_test.go::TestRFC4271RelayedRouteTooLargeForThePeerIsWithheld#2 moved. RFC 4271 Section 9.2: “If, due to the limits on the maximum size of an UPDATE message (see Section 4), a single route doesn't fit into the message, the BGP speaker MUST not advertise the route to its peers and MAY choose to log an error locally.” The changed splitter pair retains equivalent non-ADD-PATH framing. Its oversized opaque-attribute case requires an error and zero callbacks; the fitting control preserves attributes and NLRI in one output. The genuine relay pair supplies protocol-shaped input: a single route with 900 communities fits and is relayed byte-identically; 1100 communities exceed the 4096-byte destination limit and yield ok=false with neither raw nor parsed output. Both forwarding callers explicitly release and skip failed items, so failure is not later dispatched. Final ordinary-write admission also refuses an indivisible effective chunk. The corrected test tag accurately says an error, not an assertion of the exact ErrAttributesTooLarge identity. Logging is optional. Whole-producer halt records are reachability evidence, not selective size-check mutations.

### [`RFC4271-9.1.2-5`](#rfc4271-9.1.2-5)

If the AS_PATH attribute of a BGP route contains an AS loop, the BGP route should be excluded from the Phase 2 decision function (§9.1.2). Detection scans the full AS path and checks that the local autonomous system number does not appear in it. RFC 4271 writes this keyword in lower case, so the level is a recommendation and not a capitalized RFC 2119 SHOULD. The same paragraph places a speaker configured to accept routes with its own autonomous system number in the AS path outside the scope of the document. That out-of-scope case is what the allow-own-as setting selects, so a non-zero allow-own-as is not a deviation from this line.

### [`RFC4271-6.2-9`](#rfc4271-6.2-9)

If one of the Optional Parameters in the OPEN message is not recognized, then the Error Subcode MUST be set to Unsupported Optional Parameters (§6.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), stale-unit: internal/component/bgp/reactor/session_read.go moved. Independent re-judgment after 562bc0139d against RFC 4271 Section 6.2 (unrecognized Optional Parameter: Error Subcode MUST be Unsupported Optional Parameters). writeMessageHeld now passes every *message.Notification through notificationWithinPeerLimit, which returns it untouched when Notification.Len (19 + 2 + len(Data)) fits the peer's limit (4096, or 65535 when the peer advertised capability 6, bounded by writeBuf); the 2/4 here is 21 octets. TestSessionRFC4271OptionalParameterErrors (unchanged) sends an OPEN with unknown parameter type 99 over a pipe, reads the reply by its header Length and requires a NOTIFICATION whose body is exactly [2, 4] and the session Idle, so a wrong length, code or subcode fails. Negative: unknown capability 200 inside recognized type 2 reaches OpenConfirm with a KEEPALIVE. UnpackOpen changed in 79815edae5 (RFC 9072 Non-Ext OP Type 255 detection); type 99 with Optional Parameters Length 2 does not take that path. session_read.go (a27a951239) and NewSession changed in other commits; the test observes the bytes on the socket, so a change there that altered the reply turns it red rather than green.

| positive | [`TestSessionRFC4271OptionalParameterErrors`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/bgp/reactor/rfc4271_session_core4271_test.go#L36) | unit/verify | mutant, verified |

### [`RFC4271-6.2-10`](#rfc4271-6.2-10)

If one of the Optional Parameters in the OPEN message is recognized, but is malformed, then the Error Subcode MUST be set to 0 (Unspecific) (§6.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), stale-unit: internal/component/bgp/reactor/session_read.go moved. Independent re-judgment after 562bc0139d against RFC 4271 Section 6.2 (recognized but malformed Optional Parameter: Error Subcode MUST be 0, Unspecific). The NOTIFICATION is 21 octets with empty Data, so notificationWithinPeerLimit (applied in writeMessageHeld to *message.Notification only) returns it unchanged. TestSessionRFC4271OptionalParameterErrors (unchanged) requires the exact reply body [2, 0] and Idle for six malformed inputs: known capability with a wrong length, a truncated capability value, an empty capability parameter, a truncated parameter value and a truncated parameter header; the reply is read by its header Length, so header, code and subcode are all pinned. Negative: a correctly encoded Route Refresh capability reaches OpenConfirm and sends a KEEPALIVE. UnpackOpen's RFC 9072 change (79815edae5) does not reach these Optional Parameters Length values (2-5), and other-commit changes to session_read.go and NewSession would show as a red on the socket bytes, not a false green.

### [`RFC4271-6.3-5`](#rfc4271-6.3-5)

If any recognized attribute has Attribute Flags that conflict with the Attribute Type Code, then the Error Subcode MUST be set to Attribute Flags Error. The Data field MUST contain the erroneous attribute (type, length, and value). (§6.3)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Revised by RFC 7606 3(c) to treat-as-withdraw unless the attribute's specification mandates other flag handling. Flag conflicts are decided by one table (AttributeCode.FlagsConflict via validateAttributeFlags). Tagged units now cover ORIGIN (well-known), MED, AGGREGATOR and ATOMIC_AGGREGATE with conflicting Optional/Transitive bits: exact withdraw-only payload, session Established; the same attributes with specified flags keep the prefix and the value byte for byte. AGGREGATOR flags go to withdraw because RFC 7606 7.7 mandates attribute discard only for its length conditions, not for flags. Observed-red records on validateAttributeFlags both polarities.

### [`RFC4271-6.3-6`](#rfc4271-6.3-6)

If any recognized attribute has an Attribute Length that conflicts with the expected length (based on the attribute type code), then the Error Subcode MUST be set to Attribute Length Error. The Data field MUST contain the erroneous attribute (type, length, and value). (§6.3)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Revised by RFC 7606 to per-attribute outcomes. Every attribute whose length its type code fixes now has a length case against its expected length: ORIGIN (withdraw, 7.1), NEXT_HOP (withdraw, 7.3) and MED in TestSessionRFC4271RevisedAttributeErrors, ATOMIC_AGGREGATE and AGGREGATOR (attribute discard, prefix kept, 7.6/7.7) in TestRFC4271RecognizedAttributeErrorsBeyondTheFirst, and LOCAL_PREF in TestRFC4271LocalPrefLengthFromInternalPeer: an iBGP session over the real receive path, length 3 yields exactly the withdrawal of the prefix with the session still Established (7.5), length 4 before and after keeps the prefix with LOCAL_PREF byte-identical. The LOCAL_PREF buffer is otherwise valid, so only the length rule can fire. Observed-red records on validateLocalPrefAttr, validateAggregatorAttr and validateNextHopAttr, both polarities.

If any of the well-known mandatory attributes are not present, then the Error Subcode MUST be set to Missing Well-known Attribute. The Data field MUST contain the Attribute Type Code of the missing, well-known attribute. (§6.3)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), stale-unit: internal/component/bgp/reactor/rfc7947_session_first_as_test.go::firstASReceive, internal/component/bgp/reactor/session_read.go::Session.deliverReceivedMessage, internal/component/bgp/reactor/session_read.go::Session.processValidatedMessage moved. Independent current rejudgment of missing mandatory attributes. RFC7606 Section3(d) replaces the original Missing Well-known Attribute reset/subcode/Data with treat-as-withdraw; RFC4760 NEXT_HOP discretion for MP-only announcements remains. Read both complete tagged roots: Session missing ORIGIN, missing AS_PATH and missing NEXT_HOP each require exact withdrawal of203.0.113.0/24, while all-present good announcements assert that exact NLRI before and after. The distinct message root TestRFC4271MandatoryAttributesAcrossUpdateForms also remains in the whole source judgment: missing legacy NEXT_HOP despite MP_REACH must give exact TreatAsWithdraw; mixed complete and MP-only cases must give exact None. Read actual main80743 receipt870f2b5c: named TestRFC4271MandatoryAttributesAcrossUpdateForms and all three mixed_missing_legacy_next_hop/mixed_complete/MP_only children PASS, alongside the named OpenValidateHoldTime seven children and WellKnownAttributeErrors carrier; race package1.402s. This is an actual clean mixed-form control, not a producer-break discrimination record for its two tags. Read actual accepted52782 receipt491e097e and the canonical positive record: clean root and all twelve children PASS; the post-classification empty-UPDATE fault fails only the three missing-attribute children at fatal145, expected eight-byte withdrawal00 04 18 cb 00 71 00 00, actual four-byte empty00 00 00 00. Their initial good announcements and subsequent successful receive/Established/one-callback helper checks precede failure. All nine other error children PASS their full sequences. Each matching broken child's final good input is skipped. Correct TAW classification and synthesis call remain, with only output materialization replaced; later semantic NEXT_HOP/first-AS/AS4 checks gated below TAW cannot repair the lost withdrawal. Read full RFC4271 Section6.3 with RFC7606 Sections2/3 and the applicable specific clauses; historical attribute-error notification/reset wording is not reinstated. Independent source judgment remains enforced at the actual retained semantic scope, preserving both declared polarities. The firstASSession/firstASReceive fixture negotiates a two-octet eBGP receive context on net.Pipe and snapshots the semantic WireUpdate delivered to consumers. Good-input prefix assertions and successful receive/Established/exactly-one-callback checks are distinct from error-response assertions; a reset, absent callback or unchanged announcement cannot pass the withdrawal oracle. The clean named baseline supplies the complete good/bad/good survival sequence; no matching broken child whose fatal assertion stops early is credited with later recovery. Explicit configuration/capability/AS_SET/migration and other operator extensions remain supported under the default-baseline rule; no extension is removed to earn compliance. Boundary is an in-process net.Pipe Session plus copied consumer payload (and the syntax root's actual notification bytes), not a foreign TCP peer, installed Adj-RIB/Loc-RIB removal or kernel behavior. Main supplied separate scoped race30945 package PASS2.050s after the foreign CLI correction; that package result is not substituted for the named semantic receipts. Final native freshness remains main-owned. These five accepted positive records do not prove all twelve tagged covers: opposite-polarity and distinct mixed-form native discrimination remain unrecorded unless separately supplied. Native stamp must derive actual tag/test/unit/code fingerprints; all existing critical code bindings are retained with empty values and no hashes are authored. Qualified working-tree stamping and a coherent five-record/five-audit commit are legitimate; no two-commit or source=head requirement is imposed.

### [`RFC4271-6.3-8`](#rfc4271-6.3-8)

If any of the well-known mandatory attributes are not recognized, then the Error Subcode MUST be set to Unrecognized Well-known Attribute. The Data field MUST contain the unrecognized attribute (type, length, and value). (§6.3)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), stale-unit: internal/component/bgp/reactor/session_read.go moved. Independent re-judgment after 562bc0139d against RFC 4271 Section 6.3 ("If any of the well-known mandatory attributes are not recognized, then the Error Subcode MUST be set to Unrecognized Well-known Attribute. The Data field MUST contain the unrecognized attribute (type, length, and value)."). New in writeMessageHeld: a *message.Notification (the only type with Type() NOTIFICATION) goes through notificationWithinPeerLimit, which returns it untouched when Notification.Len = 19 + 2 + len(Data) fits the limit (4096, or 65535 when the peer advertised capability 6, also bounded by writeBuf), and otherwise returns a copy whose Data is Data[:limit-21] with ErrorCode and ErrorSubcode copied; WriteTo computes the header Length from the cut Data, so header length, code and subcode cannot disagree. No other message type is touched. reactor.TestRFC8654NotificationToPeerWithoutExtendedMessage pins the cut: Length field and wire size 4096, code 3 / subcode 9, Data the first 4075 octets, and the whole message when the peer advertised capability 6. TestSessionRFC4271RetainedUpdateNotifications (unchanged) uses a 6-octet attribute, so the cut never applies, and its exact body equality [3, 2, 0x50, 250, 0, 2, 0xab, 0xcd] still pins subcode and the whole attribute; negative: the same code 250 marked Optional (0xd0) is accepted and its NLRI announced. firstASReceive (ba0f3a3a7d fixture migration) still requires a successful receive, Established and exactly one received UPDATE. Edge, not a violation: an unrecognized well-known attribute over 4075 octets from a peer that did not advertise capability 6 (reachable because Ze accepts extended UPDATEs whenever it advertised the capability) now has its Data cut, so the Data no longer holds the whole attribute; RFC 8654 Section 5, which updates RFC 4271, requires that cap, and no 6.3-8 test covers that edge.

### [`RFC4271-6.3-9`](#rfc4271-6.3-9)

If the ORIGIN attribute has an undefined value, then the Error Sub- code MUST be set to Invalid Origin Attribute. The Data field MUST contain the unrecognized attribute (type, length, and value). (§6.3)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), stale-unit: internal/component/bgp/reactor/rfc7947_session_first_as_test.go::firstASReceive, internal/component/bgp/reactor/session_read.go::Session.deliverReceivedMessage, internal/component/bgp/reactor/session_read.go::Session.processValidatedMessage moved. Independent current rejudgment of undefined ORIGIN. RFC7606 Section7.1 requires treat-as-withdraw for undefined ORIGIN, replacing the historical Invalid Origin notification/Data. The isolated ORIGIN3 input has length1, correct flags, valid AS_PATH/NEXT_HOP and parseable legacy NLRI; good ORIGIN IGP produces distinct accepted-prefix assertions before and after on the same Session. Read actual accepted79203 receipt49ca6c13 and canonical positive record: clean root and all twelve children PASS; the selective length1/value>2 post-decision fault fails only origin_value at fatal145, expected eight-byte203.0.113.0/24 withdrawal, actual empty four-byte UPDATE. Initial good and helper receive/Established/one-callback checks precede it; the matching broken child's trailing good is skipped. Missing ORIGIN, flags/value0 and all ten other children PASS their complete sequences. The validator still chooses TAW; no length-only carrier or generic reset/timeout is substituted as the undefined-value proof. Read full RFC4271 Section6.3 with RFC7606 Sections2/3 and the applicable specific clauses; historical attribute-error notification/reset wording is not reinstated. Independent source judgment remains enforced at the actual retained semantic scope, preserving both declared polarities. The firstASSession/firstASReceive fixture negotiates a two-octet eBGP receive context on net.Pipe and snapshots the semantic WireUpdate delivered to consumers. Good-input prefix assertions and successful receive/Established/exactly-one-callback checks are distinct from error-response assertions; a reset, absent callback or unchanged announcement cannot pass the withdrawal oracle. The clean named baseline supplies the complete good/bad/good survival sequence; no matching broken child whose fatal assertion stops early is credited with later recovery. Explicit configuration/capability/AS_SET/migration and other operator extensions remain supported under the default-baseline rule; no extension is removed to earn compliance. Boundary is an in-process net.Pipe Session plus copied consumer payload (and the syntax root's actual notification bytes), not a foreign TCP peer, installed Adj-RIB/Loc-RIB removal or kernel behavior. Main supplied separate scoped race30945 package PASS2.050s after the foreign CLI correction; that package result is not substituted for the named semantic receipts. Final native freshness remains main-owned. These five accepted positive records do not prove all twelve tagged covers: opposite-polarity and distinct mixed-form native discrimination remain unrecorded unless separately supplied. Native stamp must derive actual tag/test/unit/code fingerprints; all existing critical code bindings are retained with empty values and no hashes are authored. Qualified working-tree stamping and a coherent five-record/five-audit commit are legitimate; no two-commit or source=head requirement is imposed.

### [`RFC4271-6.3-11`](#rfc4271-6.3-11)

The IP address in the NEXT_HOP MUST meet the following criteria to be considered semantically correct: a) It MUST NOT be the IP address of the receiving speaker. b) In the case of an EBGP, where the sender and receiver are one IP hop away from each other, either the IP address in the NEXT_HOP MUST be the sender's IP address that is used to establish the BGP connection, or the interface associated with the NEXT_HOP IP address MUST share a common subnet with the receiving BGP speaker. (§6.3)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. criterion a) receiver's own address (eBGP and iBGP) and b) off-link next hop on a one-hop eBGP session each withhold the legacy announcement on the live receive path; sender address and on-subnet third party accepted; multihop and iBGP accept off-link; interface snapshot change flips the verdict. The reaction (ignore, no NOTIFICATION) is RFC4271-6.3-2/6.3-3

### [`RFC4271-6.3-13`](#rfc4271-6.3-13)

If the UPDATE message is received from an external peer, the local system MAY check whether the leftmost (with respect to the position of octets in the protocol message) AS in the AS_PATH attribute is equal to the autonomous system number of the peer that sent the message. If the check determines this is not the case, the Error Subcode MUST be set to Malformed AS_PATH. (§6.3)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. RFC 7606 (Updates: 4271) revises this: the error is handled by treat-as-withdraw, so no NOTIFICATION, subcode or Data field is sent. an external UPDATE whose leftmost AS 65003 is not the peer AS 65002 is delivered as exactly a withdrawal of its prefix (7.2); leftmost AS equal to the peer AS announces unchanged

### [`RFC4271-6.3-14`](#rfc4271-6.3-14)

If an optional attribute is recognized, then the value of this attribute MUST be checked. If an error is detected, the attribute MUST be discarded, and the Error Subcode MUST be set to Optional Attribute Error. The Data field MUST contain the attribute (type, length, and value). (§6.3)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Revised by RFC 7606. 'If an optional attribute is recognized, then the value MUST be checked': RFC 4271's own optional attributes, MED (3 octets -> exact withdrawal, 4 kept) and AGGREGATOR (bad length -> discarded, prefix kept, the 'attribute MUST be discarded' clause), are both covered, plus COMMUNITIES and EXTENDED COMMUNITIES (withdraw per 7606 7.8/7.14) with valid values kept byte for byte. Observed-red records on validateCommunityAttr both polarities. ORIGINATOR_ID and CLUSTER_LIST value checks belong to the RFC 4456 / RFC 7606 per-attribute rows, not this generic RFC 4271 row.

### [`RFC4271-6.3-15`](#rfc4271-6.3-15)

If any attribute appears more than once in the UPDATE message, then the Error Subcode MUST be set to Malformed Attribute List (§6.3)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Revised by RFC 7606 Section 3(g): a duplicate MP_REACH_NLRI or MP_UNREACH_NLRI draws NOTIFICATION Malformed Attribute List; any other duplicate is discarded after the first. TestRFC4271UpdateMalformedAttributeList: duplicate MP_REACH_NLRI -> NOTIFICATION 3/1 read off the socket; duplicate COMMUNITY -> no NOTIFICATION, exactly one COMMUNITY left (negative). 2026-10-01 re-judge (BGP c9 judge, wrote none of these units): the MP_UNREACH clause is now tagged: TestSessionRFC7606DuplicateMPUnreachNotificationOnTheWire reads 3/1 off the wire for a duplicate MP_UNREACH_NLRI, and its fixture (ORIGIN, empty AS_PATH, two well-formed MP_UNREACH, no NLRI) records no other error, so only the duplicate check produces the reset (RFC7606-3.g-1 mutant B evidence). Dropping the mpUnreachCount operand now turns a tagged unit red. ValidateUpdateRFC7606AddPath breaks observed red for all three units.

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), stale-unit: internal/component/bgp/reactor/rfc7947_session_first_as_test.go::firstASReceive moved. RFC4271 Section6.3 retains Invalid Network Field subcode10. Read the complete tagged Session root: after a valid announced prefix, length33, truncated /24 NLRI and truncated /24 Withdrawn Routes each require exact NOTIFICATION3/10 with empty Data and Idle; the initial good-prefix assertions supply the distinct conforming scope. The same root's extended unknown well-known attribute3/2/fullData/Idle behavior remains a separately meaningful control. Both fields reach validateRFC7606IPv4/ValidateNLRISyntaxAddPath before attribute treatment. Read actual accepted94171 receipte3fc13e2 and canonical positive record: clean root/all four children PASS; changing only syntax-reset subcode10 to1 yields exactly three fatal combined-notification-body failures at200, expected03 0a, actual03 01, with no Data. Each initial good announcement, require.Error196, successful notification reader198 and type199 precedes failure. Idle201 is skipped in each broken syntax child; the unknown_well-known child PASS includes its exact3/2/offending Data and Idle checks. The fault preserves reset, and no modern attribute TAW/discard/session-survival behavior is tightened. Read full RFC4271 Section6.3 with RFC7606 Sections2/3 and the applicable specific clauses; historical attribute-error notification/reset wording is not reinstated. The malformed input then follows the reset path: receive error, successfully read NOTIFICATION, exact3/10 with empty Data, and Idle in the clean baseline. This root has an initial-good/bad/reset sequence, with no trailing good UPDATE or recovery assertion. Under the wrong-subcode fault, the exact notification-body assertion fails fatally before Idle; no later state or recovery pass is claimed, and no withdrawal-payload oracle is attributed to this root. No document obsoletes RFC 4271, so its obligations are stated where they were written.
